Apr 19
🎓 Complete OOP in Kotlin — From Absolute Beginner to Advanced
(Full Course + Interview Prep + Practice Problems)
Note: Yeh ek complete course hai — beginner se leke advanced tak, ek hi response mein. Isko bookmark karo, notes banao, aur practice karte raho! 💪

🔷 SECTION 1: FUNDAMENTALS
What is OOP? (Object-Oriented Programming)
Simple explanation: OOP ek programming style hai jisme hum real-world cheezein (objects) ko code mein represent karte hain.

Socho aise — duniya mein sab kuch objects hain:

🚗 Car ek object hai — uske paas color, brand, speed hain (properties) aur woh chalna, rukna, horn bajana jaanta hai (behaviors)
📱 Phone ek object hai — uske paas battery, screen size hain aur woh call karna, message bhejana jaanta hai
OOP mein hum code ko usi tarah organize karte hain jaise real world mein cheezein hoti hain.

Why OOP is Used — Real-World Problems it Solves
Problem (Without OOP)	Solution (With OOP)
Code sab jagah scattered hota hai	Related code ek jagah (class mein) bundled hota hai
Ek change sab jagah tod deta hai	Encapsulation se changes isolated rehte hain
Same code baar baar likhna padta hai	Inheritance se code reuse hota hai
Large codebase samajhna mushkil	Objects real-world concepts mirror karte hain
Team collaboration mushkil	Clear boundaries with classes/interfaces
Real example: Socho Amazon ka codebase — millions of lines. Without OOP, yeh ek spaghetti mess hota. OOP ki wajah se User, Product, Order, Payment — sab alag alag manage hote hain.

Procedural vs OOP — Clear Comparison
Procedural Style (Old Way):
kotlin
// Sab kuch functions mein, koi structure nahi
var carBrand = "Toyota"
var carSpeed = 0

fun accelerate() {
    carSpeed += 10
    println("$carBrand speed: $carSpeed")
}

fun brake() {
    carSpeed -= 10
    println("$carBrand speed: $carSpeed")
}

// Agar 5 cars hain toh? 5 baar sab likhna padega 😫
OOP Style (Modern Way):
kotlin
class Car(val brand: String) {
    var speed = 0

    fun accelerate() {
        speed += 10
        println("$brand speed: $speed")
    }

    fun brake() {
        speed -= 10
        println("$brand speed: $speed")
    }
}

// 100 cars bhi ban sakti hain ek hi class se!
val car1 = Car("Toyota")
val car2 = Car("BMW")
val car3 = Car("Ferrari")
Comparison Table:
Aspect	Procedural	OOP
Focus	Functions/Actions	Objects/Data
Data	Global, open	Encapsulated
Reusability	Copy-paste	Inheritance
Scalability	Poor	Excellent
Real-world mapping	Difficult	Natural
Examples	C, Pascal	Kotlin, Java, Python
Key OOP Principles Overview (4 Pillars)
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │           OOP KE 4 PILLARS               │
        ├──────────────┬──────────────┬────────────┤
        │              │              │            │
   ENCAPSULATION  INHERITANCE  POLYMORPHISM  ABSTRACTION
        │              │              │            │
   Data ko        Parent se     Ek naam,      Complexity
   protect karo   inherit karo  anek roop     chupaao
        └──────────────┴──────────────┴────────────┘
Encapsulation — Data ko band karo, safely expose karo
Inheritance — Parent class se properties/methods lena
Polymorphism — Same method, alag behavior
Abstraction — "Kya karta hai" dikhao, "Kaise karta hai" chupaao
🔷 SECTION 2: CORE CONCEPTS (DETAILED)
1. 🏗️ CLASS AND OBJECT
What is it?
Class ek blueprint/template hai — jaise ghar ka naksha (map). Object us blueprint se bana actual ghar hai.

Class = Recipe (Biryani ki recipe)
Object = Actual Biryani jo plate mein hai 🍛
Why do we use it?
Problem: Agar 100 students ka data store karna ho toh 100 alag variables likhoge? No way!

Solution: Ek Student class banao, 100 objects banao — done!

How it Works Internally
JVM mein jab class define karta hai:

Class definition .class bytecode mein compile hoti hai
Jab val s = Student() likhte ho, JVM heap memory mein ek naya object allocate karta hai
Object ka reference stack memory mein store hota hai
s actually ek reference variable hai — actual object heap pe hai
Stack Memory          Heap Memory
┌──────────┐         ┌─────────────────┐
│  s ──────┼────────▶│ Student Object  │
│          │         │  name: "Rahul"  │
│          │         │  age: 20        │
└──────────┘         └─────────────────┘
Syntax in Kotlin
kotlin
// Class definition
class ClassName {
    // Properties (data)
    var property: Type = value
    
    // Methods (behavior)
    fun methodName() {
        // code
    }
}

// Object creation
val objectName = ClassName()
Examples
Basic Example:

kotlin
class Dog {
    var name: String = ""
    var breed: String = ""
    var age: Int = 0
    
    fun bark() {
        println("$name says: Woof! Woof! 🐕")
    }
    
    fun fetch() {
        println("$name is fetching the ball!")
    }
}

fun main() {
    val dog1 = Dog()
    dog1.name = "Bruno"
    dog1.breed = "Labrador"
    dog1.age = 3
    dog1.bark()      // Bruno says: Woof! Woof! 🐕
    dog1.fetch()     // Bruno is fetching the ball!
    
    val dog2 = Dog()
    dog2.name = "Max"
    dog2.bark()      // Max says: Woof! Woof! 🐕
}
Intermediate Example:

kotlin
class BankAccount {
    var accountNumber: String = ""
    var holderName: String = ""
    private var balance: Double = 0.0   // private — bahar se access nahi
    
    fun deposit(amount: Double) {
        if (amount > 0) {
            balance += amount
            println("₹$amount deposited. New balance: ₹$balance")
        } else {
            println("Invalid amount!")
        }
    }
    
    fun withdraw(amount: Double) {
        when {
            amount <= 0 -> println("Invalid amount!")
            amount > balance -> println("Insufficient funds!")
            else -> {
                balance -= amount
                println("₹$amount withdrawn. Remaining: ₹$balance")
            }
        }
    }
    
    fun getBalance(): Double = balance
}

fun main() {
    val account = BankAccount()
    account.accountNumber = "SBI001"
    account.holderName = "Rahul Sharma"
    account.deposit(50000.0)    // ₹50000.0 deposited
    account.withdraw(15000.0)   // ₹15000.0 withdrawn
    println("Balance: ₹${account.getBalance()}")  // ₹35000.0
}
Real-World Android Example:

kotlin
// Android mein ViewModel jaisi class
class UserViewModel {
    private var currentUser: User? = null
    private var isLoading: Boolean = false
    
    data class User(val id: Int, val name: String, val email: String)
    
    fun loadUser(userId: Int) {
        isLoading = true
        println("Loading user $userId...")
        // Simulate API call
        currentUser = User(userId, "Rahul", "rahul@example.com")
        isLoading = false
        println("User loaded: ${currentUser?.name}")
    }
    
    fun getCurrentUser(): User? = currentUser
    fun isUserLoading(): Boolean = isLoading
}

fun main() {
    val viewModel = UserViewModel()
    viewModel.loadUser(101)
    println(viewModel.getCurrentUser())
}
Real-Life Analogy
🏭 Class = Car Manufacturing Factory Blueprint

Factory ke paas ek master design (class) hai — engine kahan hoga, wheels kitne, doors kaise. Is design se lakhon cars (objects) banai jaati hain. Har car alag color ki ho sakti hai, alag features ke saath — lekin sab same blueprint se bani hain.

Common Mistakes
kotlin
// ❌ MISTAKE 1: Class use karne se pehle object banana bhool gaye
class Cat {
    var name = "Kitty"
    fun meow() = println("Meow!")
}

// fun main() { meow() }  // ERROR! Object banao pehle

// ✅ CORRECT
fun main() {
    val cat = Cat()
    cat.meow()
}

// ❌ MISTAKE 2: Class name lowercase mein
// class myclass { }  // Convention violation!

// ✅ CORRECT: PascalCase
class MyClass { }

// ❌ MISTAKE 3: Private members ko bahar access karna
class Person {
    private var secret = "I am Batman"
}
// val p = Person()
// println(p.secret)  // ERROR! Cannot access 'secret'
Best Practices
Single Responsibility Principle — Ek class ek kaam kare
PascalCase class names ke liye (UserRepository, not userrepository)
Properties ko private rakho, methods se access do
Classes chhoti rakho — 200-300 lines se zyada nahi
Meaningful names do — User not U, calculateTax() not calc()
Interview Questions
Basic:

Q: Class aur Object mein kya difference hai?
A: Class ek blueprint/template hai, Object us blueprint ka ek instance hai. Class once define hoti hai, Objects multiple ban sakte hain.
Intermediate:

Q: Kotlin mein class instantiate karne ke liye new keyword kyun nahi use karte?
A: Kotlin ne new keyword remove kar diya for cleaner syntax. ClassName() directly object create karta hai. Internally kotlin compiler new call hi karta hai JVM ke liye.
Advanced:

Q: JVM mein Object kahan store hota hai?
A: Object Heap memory mein store hota hai. Reference variable Stack memory mein. Jab object ki koi reference nahi rehti, Garbage Collector usse Heap se remove kar deta hai.
Coding Practice
Q1: Ek Rectangle class banao jisme width aur height properties hon aur area() aur perimeter() methods hon.

kotlin
class Rectangle(val width: Double, val height: Double) {
    fun area(): Double = width * height
    fun perimeter(): Double = 2 * (width + height)
    
    override fun toString(): String {
        return "Rectangle(${width}x${height}) | Area: ${area()} | Perimeter: ${perimeter()}"
    }
}

fun main() {
    val r1 = Rectangle(5.0, 3.0)
    println(r1)  // Rectangle(5.0x3.0) | Area: 15.0 | Perimeter: 16.0
    
    val r2 = Rectangle(10.0, 7.5)
    println(r2)
}
2. 🔨 CONSTRUCTORS (Primary & Secondary)
What is it?
Constructor ek special function hai jo object create hone ke time automatically call hota hai. Iska kaam object ko initialize karna hai.

Constructor = Ghar banana shuru karne ke time jo first setup hota hai
(foundation, walls, roof) — automatically hota hai
Why do we use it?
Problem: Har baar object banane ke baad manually properties set karna padta hai — tedious aur error-prone.

kotlin
// Without constructor — boring aur risky
val dog = Dog()
dog.name = "Bruno"    // bhool gaya toh?
dog.breed = "Lab"     // bhool gaya toh?
dog.age = 3           // bhool gaya toh?
Solution: Constructor mein sab kuch pass karo ek baar mein!

kotlin
// With constructor — clean!
val dog = Dog("Bruno", "Lab", 3)
How it Works Internally
ClassName(args) call hota hai
JVM Heap pe memory allocate karta hai
Constructor body execute hoti hai (initialization)
Reference return hoti hai
Primary Constructor — class header mein hota hai, JVM <init> method se map hota hai Secondary Constructor — class body mein hota hai, constructor keyword se

Syntax in Kotlin
kotlin
// PRIMARY CONSTRUCTOR — class header mein
class Person(val name: String, val age: Int) {
    // Properties auto-created with val/var
}

// PRIMARY CONSTRUCTOR with init block
class Person(val name: String, val age: Int) {
    init {
        // Initialization logic — constructor body
        println("Person created: $name, age $age")
        require(age > 0) { "Age must be positive!" }
    }
}

// SECONDARY CONSTRUCTOR — class body mein
class Person {
    var name: String
    var age: Int
    
    constructor(name: String, age: Int) {
        this.name = name
        this.age = age
    }
    
    constructor(name: String) : this(name, 0) {
        // Secondary must call primary
    }
}
Examples
Basic Example — Primary Constructor:

kotlin
class Student(val name: String, val rollNo: Int, val grade: String) {
    
    init {
        println("✅ New student registered: $name (Roll: $rollNo, Grade: $grade)")
    }
    
    fun introduce() {
        println("Hi! I'm $name, Roll No: $rollNo, studying in Grade $grade")
    }
}

fun main() {
    val s1 = Student("Priya", 101, "10th")    // ✅ New student registered: Priya...
    val s2 = Student("Arjun", 102, "11th")    // ✅ New student registered: Arjun...
    
    s1.introduce()   // Hi! I'm Priya, Roll No: 101, studying in Grade 10th
    s2.introduce()
}
Intermediate Example — Default Parameters + Secondary Constructor:

kotlin
class Pizza(
    val size: String,
    val crust: String = "Thin",        // Default value
    val extraCheese: Boolean = false   // Default value
) {
    val toppings = mutableListOf<String>()
    var price: Double = 0.0
    
    init {
        price = when (size) {
            "Small" -> 199.0
            "Medium" -> 299.0
            "Large" -> 399.0
            else -> 249.0
        }
        if (extraCheese) price += 50.0
        println("🍕 Pizza created: $size | Crust: $crust | Price: ₹$price")
    }
    
    // Secondary constructor
    constructor(size: String, toppingList: List<String>) : this(size) {
        toppings.addAll(toppingList)
        price += toppingList.size * 30  // ₹30 per topping
        println("Added toppings: $toppingList | Updated price: ₹$price")
    }
    
    fun addTopping(topping: String) {
        toppings.add(topping)
        price += 30
    }
    
    fun printOrder() {
        println("Order: $size $crust crust pizza | Toppings: $toppings | Total: ₹$price")
    }
}

fun main() {
    // Default parameters use karna
    val p1 = Pizza("Large")                           // Price: ₹399
    val p2 = Pizza("Medium", "Thick", true)           // Price: ₹349
    val p3 = Pizza("Small", listOf("Mushroom", "Corn")) // Secondary constructor
    
    p1.addTopping("Paneer")
    p1.printOrder()
}
Real-World Android Example:

kotlin
// Android RecyclerView Adapter jaisi class
class ProductAdapter(
    private val products: List<Product>,
    private val onItemClick: (Product) -> Unit,
    private val showDiscount: Boolean = true
) {
    data class Product(
        val id: Int,
        val name: String,
        val price: Double,
        val discount: Int = 0
    )
    
    init {
        println("Adapter initialized with ${products.size} products")
        validate()
    }
    
    private fun validate() {
        require(products.isNotEmpty()) { "Product list cannot be empty!" }
    }
    
    fun getItemCount() = products.size
    
    fun getEffectivePrice(product: Product): Double {
        return if (showDiscount && product.discount > 0) {
            product.price * (1 - product.discount / 100.0)
        } else {
            product.price
        }
    }
    
    fun renderItem(position: Int) {
        val product = products[position]
        val price = getEffectivePrice(product)
        println("${product.name} - ₹$price ${if (showDiscount && product.discount > 0) "(${product.discount}% off)" else ""}")
    }
}

fun main() {
    val products = listOf(
        ProductAdapter.Product(1, "Phone", 15000.0, 10),
        ProductAdapter.Product(2, "Laptop", 55000.0, 5),
        ProductAdapter.Product(3, "Earbuds", 2000.0)
    )
    
    val adapter = ProductAdapter(products, { p -> println("Clicked: ${p.name}") })
    
    for (i in 0 until adapter.getItemCount()) {
        adapter.renderItem(i)
    }
}
Real-Life Analogy
🏠 Constructor = Ghar ka Foundation

Jab ghar banate ho, pehle foundation daalte ho — ye automatically hota hai, tum ignore nahi kar sakte. Primary constructor = main foundation. Secondary constructor = alag type ke ghar ke liye alternate foundation — lekin main foundation ko zaroor call karta hai!

Common Mistakes
kotlin
// ❌ MISTAKE 1: init block mein heavy operations
class MyClass(val id: Int) {
    init {
        // Thread.sleep(5000) // Network call ya heavy work — NEVER in init!
        // Yeh main thread block kar dega
    }
}

// ❌ MISTAKE 2: Secondary constructor primary ko call nahi karta
class Vehicle {
    val type: String
    // constructor(type: String) { this.type = type }  // Primary nahi hai, error!
    
    // ✅ Correct — primary define karo ya this() call karo
    constructor(type: String, speed: Int) : this() // ERROR if no primary!
}

// ✅ CORRECT
class Vehicle(val type: String) {
    var speed: Int = 0
    constructor(type: String, speed: Int) : this(type) {
        this.speed = speed
    }
}

// ❌ MISTAKE 3: val/var property ko init mein reassign karna
class Demo(val name: String) {
    init {
        // name = "Changed"  // ERROR! val cannot be reassigned
    }
}
Best Practices
Primary constructor prefer karo — cleaner syntax
Default values use karo instead of multiple secondary constructors
init block mein validation karo — require(), check() use karo
Heavy operations constructor mein mat karo — lazy initialization use karo
Constructor parameters ko val/var banao — auto property creation
Interview Questions
Basic:

Q: Primary aur Secondary constructor mein kya difference hai?
A: Primary constructor class header mein hota hai, Secondary class body mein constructor keyword se. Secondary must call primary using this().
Intermediate:

Q: init block kab execute hota hai?
A: Primary constructor ke saath, object creation ke time pe. Multiple init blocks ho sakte hain — top to bottom order mein execute hote hain. Secondary constructor ke baad bhi init execute hota hai.
Advanced:

Q: Kotlin mein init blocks aur secondary constructors ka execution order kya hai?
A:
kotlin
class Example(val x: Int) {
    init { println("Init 1: x = $x") }          // 1st
    
    val y = x * 2.also { println("Property y: $it") }  // 2nd
    
    init { println("Init 2: y = $y") }          // 3rd
    
    constructor(x: Int, z: Int) : this(x) {
        println("Secondary: z = $z")             // 4th (after all inits)
    }
}
// Output order: Init 1 → Property y → Init 2 → Secondary
Coding Practice
Q1: Ek Employee class banao with primary constructor (name, department, salary). Secondary constructor sirf name se bana sake (default department = "Intern", salary = 10000).

kotlin
class Employee(
    val name: String,
    val department: String = "Intern",
    var salary: Double = 10000.0
) {
    init {
        println("Employee hired: $name | Dept: $department | Salary: ₹$salary")
    }
    
    constructor(name: String) : this(name, "Intern", 10000.0)
    
    fun appraise(percentage: Double) {
        val hike = salary * percentage / 100
        salary += hike
        println("$name got ${percentage}% hike! New salary: ₹$salary")
    }
    
    override fun toString() = "Employee($name, $department, ₹$salary)"
}

fun main() {
    val e1 = Employee("Rahul", "Engineering", 75000.0)
    val e2 = Employee("Priya")  // Secondary constructor
    
    e1.appraise(15.0)
    println(e2)
}
3. 📦 PROPERTIES AND METHODS
What is it?
Properties = Object ka data (state) — "Kya hai object ke paas?" Methods = Object ka behavior (actions) — "Object kya kar sakta hai?"

Person Object:
├── Properties: name, age, email, phone     (DATA)
└── Methods: eat(), sleep(), work(), talk() (BEHAVIOR)
Why do we use it?
Properties aur methods milke ek complete, self-contained unit banate hain jiske paas apna data hai aur apne actions bhi.

How it Works Internally
Kotlin Properties actually Java ke getter/setter ke upar ek layer hain:

kotlin
var name: String = "Rahul"
// Kotlin internally banata hai:
// private String _name = "Rahul";
// public String getName() { return _name; }
// public void setName(String value) { _name = value; }
Custom Getter/Setter:

kotlin
var age: Int = 0
    get() = field           // field = backing field
    set(value) {
        field = if (value >= 0) value else 0
    }
Syntax in Kotlin
kotlin
class ClassName {
    // Property with type and default value
    var mutableProp: String = "default"
    val immutableProp: Int = 42
    
    // Property with custom getter
    val doubled: Int
        get() = mutableProp.length * 2
    
    // Property with getter AND setter
    var validated: Int = 0
        get() = field
        set(value) {
            field = if (value >= 0) value else throw IllegalArgumentException("Must be positive")
        }
    
    // Method
    fun doSomething(): ReturnType {
        return someValue
    }
    
    // Extension-style computed property
    val summary: String
        get() = "Prop: $mutableProp, Doubled: $doubled"
}
Examples
Basic Example:

kotlin
class Circle(val radius: Double) {
    // Computed properties — no backing field needed
    val area: Double
        get() = Math.PI * radius * radius
    
    val circumference: Double
        get() = 2 * Math.PI * radius
    
    val diameter: Double
        get() = radius * 2
    
    fun describe() {
        println("""
            📐 Circle Stats:
            Radius: $radius
            Diameter: $diameter
            Area: ${"%.2f".format(area)}
            Circumference: ${"%.2f".format(circumference)}
        """.trimIndent())
    }
}

fun main() {
    val c = Circle(7.0)
    c.describe()
    println("Is large circle: ${c.radius > 5}")
}
Intermediate Example — Custom Getters/Setters:

kotlin
class Temperature(initialCelsius: Double) {
    
    var celsius: Double = initialCelsius
        set(value) {
            require(value >= -273.15) { "Temperature below absolute zero!" }
            field = value
            println("Temperature set to ${value}°C")
        }
    
    // Computed property — backed by celsius
    val fahrenheit: Double
        get() = (celsius * 9 / 5) + 32
    
    val kelvin: Double
        get() = celsius + 273.15
    
    val state: String
        get() = when {
            celsius <= 0 -> "❄️ Ice"
            celsius < 100 -> "💧 Water"
            else -> "♨️ Steam"
        }
    
    fun printAll() {
        println("${celsius}°C = ${fahrenheit}°F = ${kelvin}K | State: $state")
    }
}

fun main() {
    val temp = Temperature(25.0)
    temp.printAll()     // 25.0°C = 77.0°F = 298.15K | State: 💧 Water
    
    temp.celsius = 100.0
    temp.printAll()     // 100.0°C = 212.0°F = 373.15K | State: ♨️ Steam
    
    temp.celsius = -10.0
    temp.printAll()     // -10.0°C | State: ❄️ Ice
}
Real-World Android Example:

kotlin
// Android ViewModel jaisi class — LiveData pattern simulate
class CartViewModel {
    
    private val _items = mutableListOf<CartItem>()
    
    data class CartItem(
        val productId: Int,
        val name: String,
        val price: Double,
        var quantity: Int = 1
    )
    
    // Read-only access to items
    val items: List<CartItem>
        get() = _items.toList()
    
    // Computed properties
    val itemCount: Int
        get() = _items.sumOf { it.quantity }
    
    val subtotal: Double
        get() = _items.sumOf { it.price * it.quantity }
    
    val tax: Double
        get() = subtotal * 0.18  // 18% GST
    
    val total: Double
        get() = subtotal + tax
    
    val isEmpty: Boolean
        get() = _items.isEmpty()
    
    // Methods
    fun addItem(item: CartItem) {
        val existing = _items.find { it.productId == item.productId }
        if (existing != null) {
            existing.quantity++
            println("${item.name} quantity increased to ${existing.quantity}")
        } else {
            _items.add(item)
            println("${item.name} added to cart")
        }
    }
    
    fun removeItem(productId: Int) {
        val removed = _items.removeAll { it.productId == productId }
        if (removed) println("Item removed from cart")
    }
    
    fun printSummary() {
        println("=== 🛒 Cart Summary ===")
        _items.forEach { println("  ${it.name} x${it.quantity} = ₹${it.price * it.quantity}") }
        println("Subtotal: ₹$subtotal | GST: ₹$tax | Total: ₹$total")
    }
}

fun main() {
    val cart = CartViewModel()
    cart.addItem(CartViewModel.CartItem(1, "Phone", 15000.0))
    cart.addItem(CartViewModel.CartItem(2, "Case", 500.0))
    cart.addItem(CartViewModel.CartItem(1, "Phone", 15000.0))  // Quantity increases
    cart.printSummary()
}
Real-Life Analogy
🚗 Properties = Car ke Specs (color, speed, fuel), Methods = Car ke Actions (start, brake, honk)

Custom getter = Odometer reading calculate karna har baar fresh (real-time)
Custom setter = Speed limit check karna before setting speed

Common Mistakes
kotlin
// ❌ MISTAKE 1: Backing field use karna bhool gaye
class Wrong {
    var name: String = ""
        set(value) {
            name = value  // ❌ INFINITE RECURSION! setter calls setter again
        }
}

// ✅ CORRECT: field keyword
class Correct {
    var name: String = ""
        set(value) {
            field = value  // ✅ field = backing field reference
        }
}

// ❌ MISTAKE 2: val property ko set karna
class Immutable {
    val id: Int = 1
}
// val obj = Immutable()
// obj.id = 2  // ERROR! Val cannot be reassigned

// ❌ MISTAKE 3: Heavy computation in getter (called repeatedly)
class Bad {
    val result: List<Int>
        get() = (1..1000000).toList()  // ❌ Million items created EVERY time!
}

// ✅ CORRECT: Lazy initialization
class Good {
    val result: List<Int> by lazy { (1..1000000).toList() }  // Only once!
}
Best Practices
val prefer karo jab property change nahi hogi
Computed properties use karo related values ke liye instead of methods with no params
Custom setters mein validation karo — fail fast
Heavy computations ke liye lazy use karo
Private properties underscore se start karo — _items, _userId
4. 🔒 VISIBILITY MODIFIERS
What is it?
Visibility Modifiers control karte hain ki class, property, ya method kahan se accessible hai.

Like a building ke different rooms:
🏠 public    = Main door — sab andar aa sakte hain
🏠 private   = Master bedroom — sirf owner andar aa sakta hai
🏠 protected = Family room — family (subclasses) andar aa sakti hai
🏠 internal  = Office — same company ke log aa sakte hain
Why do we use it?
Information Hiding: Jo zarurat nahi, woh expose mat karo. Isse:

Internal implementation change ho sakti hai bina breaking changes ke
Accidental misuse prevent hoti hai
Security improve hoti hai
Syntax in Kotlin
Modifier	Where visible
public (default)	Everywhere
private	Same class only
protected	Same class + subclasses
internal	Same module
kotlin
class AccessDemo {
    public val publicProp = "Anyone can see"         // Default
    private val privateProp = "Only I can see"
    protected val protectedProp = "Me + my children"
    internal val internalProp = "Same module only"
    
    public fun publicMethod() = println(publicProp)
    private fun privateMethod() = println(privateProp)
    protected fun protectedMethod() = println(protectedProp)
    internal fun internalMethod() = println(internalProp)
}
Examples
Basic Example:

kotlin
class Person(
    val name: String,           // public — bahar se access kar sakte hain
    private val aadhaar: String, // private — sirf class ke andar
    protected val age: Int       // protected — subclasses mein bhi
) {
    private var loginAttempts = 0
    
    fun greet() {
        println("Hello, I'm $name")
        // Can access private inside class
        println("Aadhaar (private): $aadhaar")
    }
    
    private fun logActivity() {
        loginAttempts++
        println("Login attempt #$loginAttempts")
    }
    
    fun login(pin: String): Boolean {
        logActivity()  // private method — callable inside
        return pin == "1234"
    }
}

class Employee(name: String, aadhaar: String, age: Int) : Person(name, aadhaar, age) {
    fun showAge() {
        println("Age: $age")      // ✅ protected — accessible in subclass
        // println(aadhaar)       // ❌ private — NOT accessible
    }
}

fun main() {
    val p = Person("Rahul", "1234-5678-9012", 25)
    println(p.name)       // ✅ public
    // println(p.aadhaar) // ❌ private — ERROR
    // println(p.age)     // ❌ protected — accessible only in subclass
    p.greet()
    p.login("1234")
}
Real-World Example:

kotlin
class AuthService {
    private val validApiKeys = setOf("key_abc123", "key_xyz789")
    private var requestCount = 0
    
    internal fun validateKey(apiKey: String): Boolean {
        requestCount++
        return apiKey in validApiKeys
    }
    
    private fun logRequest(endpoint: String) {
        println("[LOG] Request #$requestCount to $endpoint")
    }
    
    fun handleRequest(apiKey: String, endpoint: String): String {
        logRequest(endpoint)  // private — internal use only
        
        return if (validateKey(apiKey)) {  // internal method
            "✅ Request authorized for $endpoint"
        } else {
            "❌ Unauthorized access attempt!"
        }
    }
}

fun main() {
    val auth = AuthService()
    println(auth.handleRequest("key_abc123", "/api/users"))   // ✅
    println(auth.handleRequest("invalid_key", "/api/data"))   // ❌
}
Common Mistakes
kotlin
// ❌ MISTAKE 1: Sab kuch public rakhna
class Mistake {
    var internalState = "should be private"  // Leakage!
    var helperCount = 0                       // Implementation detail!
}

// ✅ CORRECT
class Correct {
    private var internalState = "protected"
    private var helperCount = 0
    
    fun doWork() {
        internalState = "working"
        helperCount++
    }
}

// ❌ MISTAKE 2: Protected ko public samajhna
open class Parent {
    protected val secret = "only family"
}

fun main() {
    val p = Parent()
    // println(p.secret)  // ❌ ERROR — protected not accessible from outside
}
Interview Questions
Basic:

Q: Kotlin ka default visibility modifier kya hai?
A: public — agar koi modifier nahi likha toh automatically public.
Advanced:

Q: internal modifier kab use karte hain?
A: Jab ek module (library/SDK) ke andar sab jagah accessible chahiye, lekin library use karne wale users ko expose nahi karna. Android library development mein common hai.
5. 🔐 ENCAPSULATION
What is it?
Encapsulation = Data + Methods ko ek class mein bundle karna + Data ko private rakhna + Controlled access dena via methods.

Capsule = Dawaai (medicine)
Tum tablet ke andar ki chemistry nahi dekhte, bas tablet lete ho.
Waise hi Encapsulation mein — internal data chupaao, clean interface do.
Why do we use it?
Data Protection — Koi bhi raw data directly change na kar sake
Validation — Data set karne se pehle check kar sako
Flexibility — Internal implementation change karo bina outside code todhe
Abstraction support — Complexity chupaao
How it Works Internally
Without Encapsulation:
account.balance = -50000  // Direct access — disaster!

With Encapsulation:
account.deposit(-50000)   // Method validates: "Amount must be positive!"
Examples
Basic Encapsulation:

kotlin
class BankAccount(
    val accountNumber: String,
    private var _balance: Double = 0.0,
    private var _pin: String
) {
    // Read-only property
    val balance: Double
        get() = _balance
    
    // Controlled deposit
    fun deposit(amount: Double): Boolean {
        if (amount <= 0) {
            println("❌ Invalid deposit amount!")
            return false
        }
        _balance += amount
        println("✅ Deposited ₹$amount | Balance: ₹$_balance")
        return true
    }
    
    // Controlled withdrawal with PIN
    fun withdraw(amount: Double, pin: String): Boolean {
        if (!verifyPin(pin)) {
            println("❌ Wrong PIN!")
            return false
        }
        if (amount > _balance) {
            println("❌ Insufficient balance!")
            return false
        }
        if (amount <= 0) {
            println("❌ Invalid amount!")
            return false
        }
        _balance -= amount
        println("✅ Withdrew ₹$amount | Remaining: ₹$_balance")
        return true
    }
    
    // PIN change with old PIN verification
    fun changePin(oldPin: String, newPin: String): Boolean {
        if (!verifyPin(oldPin)) {
            println("❌ Wrong old PIN!")
            return false
        }
        if (newPin.length != 4 || !newPin.all { it.isDigit() }) {
            println("❌ PIN must be 4 digits!")
            return false
        }
        _pin = newPin
        println("✅ PIN changed successfully!")
        return true
    }
    
    private fun verifyPin(pin: String) = pin == _pin
    
    override fun toString() = "Account[$accountNumber] Balance: ₹$_balance"
}

fun main() {
    val acc = BankAccount("SBI001", 10000.0, "1234")
    
    acc.deposit(5000.0)           // ✅ Deposited ₹5000
    acc.deposit(-100.0)           // ❌ Invalid
    acc.withdraw(3000.0, "1234")  // ✅ Withdrew
    acc.withdraw(20000.0, "1234") // ❌ Insufficient
    acc.withdraw(1000.0, "9999")  // ❌ Wrong PIN
    
    // acc._balance = 1000000.0   // ❌ Not possible! Encapsulated!
    println(acc.balance)           // ✅ Read-only access
    
    acc.changePin("1234", "5678")
    acc.withdraw(500.0, "5678")   // ✅ New PIN works
}
Real-World Example — User Profile:

kotlin
class UserProfile(email: String, username: String) {
    
    private var _email: String = ""
    private var _username: String = ""
    private var _followersCount: Int = 0
    private val _posts = mutableListOf<String>()
    
    // Validated email property
    var email: String
        get() = _email
        set(value) {
            require(value.contains("@") && value.contains(".")) { "Invalid email!" }
            _email = value.lowercase()
        }
    
    // Validated username
    var username: String
        get() = _username
        set(value) {
            require(value.length in 3..20) { "Username must be 3-20 chars!" }
            require(value.all { it.isLetterOrDigit() || it == '_' }) { "Invalid chars!" }
            _username = value.lowercase()
        }
    
    // Read-only computed
    val followersCount: Int get() = _followersCount
    val postCount: Int get() = _posts.size
    val posts: List<String> get() = _posts.toList()
    
    init {
        this.email = email         // Triggers validation!
        this.username = username   // Triggers validation!
    }
    
    fun addPost(content: String) {
        require(content.isNotBlank()) { "Post cannot be empty!" }
        require(content.length <= 2200) { "Post too long!" }
        _posts.add(content)
        println("✅ Post added! Total: $postCount posts")
    }
    
    fun gainFollower() {
        _followersCount++
        // Can't be manipulated from outside!
    }
    
    fun printProfile() {
        println("""
            👤 @$_username | 📧 $_email
            Posts: $postCount | Followers: $followersCount
        """.trimIndent())
    }
}

fun main() {
    val user = UserProfile("Rahul@Gmail.Com", "Rahul_Dev")
    user.addPost("My first Kotlin post! 🎉")
    user.gainFollower()
    user.printProfile()
    
    // user._followersCount = 1000000  // ❌ Can't cheat!
    // user._posts.clear()             // ❌ Can't access!
}
Real-Life Analogy
🏧 ATM Machine = Perfect Encapsulation Example

Internal mechanism (cash counting, network calls) = Private
PIN validation = Private method
Cash dispenser slot = Public interface
Tum ATM ke andar haath nahi daal sakte — sirf slot se kaam karo!
Interview Questions
Tricky:

Q: Kotlin mein data class ke properties automatically public rehti hain — kya yeh encapsulation violate karta hai?
A: Depends on use case. Data classes mostly DTOs (Data Transfer Objects) ke liye hote hain jahan data expose karna intended hai. For sensitive classes, normal class with private properties + getters prefer karo.
6. 👨‍👧 INHERITANCE
What is it?
Inheritance ek mechanism hai jisme ek class (child/subclass) doosri class (parent/superclass) ki properties aur methods inherit (lena) karti hai.

Animal (Parent)
├── Dog (Child) — inherits: name, eat(), sleep()
├── Cat (Child) — inherits: name, eat(), sleep()
└── Bird (Child) — inherits: name, eat(), sleep()

Sab animals apne extra features add karte hain (bark, meow, fly)
Why do we use it?
Problem: Without inheritance, har class mein same code likhna padega:

kotlin
// Without inheritance — code duplication nightmare!
class Dog { var name = ""; fun eat() = println("Eating"); fun bark() = println("Woof") }
class Cat { var name = ""; fun eat() = println("Eating"); fun meow() = println("Meow") }
class Bird { var name = ""; fun eat() = println("Eating"); fun fly() = println("Flying") }
// eat() ek baar fix karo, sab jagah fix ho — not possible!
Solution: Inheritance with open keyword!

How it Works Internally
┌─────────────────────┐
│   Animal (Parent)   │
│   name: String      │
│   eat()             │
│   sleep()           │
└──────────┬──────────┘
           │ extends
    ┌──────┴──────┐
    │             │
┌───▼───┐    ┌───▼───┐
│  Dog  │    │  Cat  │
│ bark()│    │meow() │
│ fetch()    │purr() │
└───────┘    └───────┘

Dog object mein BOTH Dog + Animal members hain
Syntax in Kotlin
kotlin
// Kotlin mein classes by default FINAL hoti hain (not inheritable)
// open keyword se inheritable banate hain

open class Animal(val name: String) {
    open fun sound() { println("$name makes a sound") }  // open = overridable
    fun eat() { println("$name is eating") }              // not open = NOT overridable
}

class Dog(name: String) : Animal(name) {
    override fun sound() { println("$name says: Woof!") }  // override
    fun fetch() { println("$name fetches the ball!") }
}
Examples
Basic Example:

kotlin
open class Vehicle(
    val brand: String,
    val model: String,
    protected var speed: Int = 0
) {
    open val fuelType: String = "Petrol"
    
    open fun accelerate(by: Int) {
        speed += by
        println("$brand $model accelerating to $speed km/h")
    }
    
    open fun brake() {
        speed = maxOf(0, speed - 20)
        println("$brand $model braking! Speed: $speed km/h")
    }
    
    fun describe() = "$brand $model ($fuelType) | Speed: $speed"
}

class ElectricCar(brand: String, model: String) : Vehicle(brand, model) {
    override val fuelType: String = "Electric"
    private var batteryLevel: Int = 100
    
    override fun accelerate(by: Int) {
        batteryLevel -= by / 10  // Battery drains
        super.accelerate(by)     // Call parent method!
        println("Battery: $batteryLevel%")
    }
    
    fun charge() {
        batteryLevel = 100
        println("$brand $model fully charged! 🔋")
    }
}

class Truck(brand: String, model: String) : Vehicle(brand, model) {
    var cargoWeight: Int = 0
    
    override fun accelerate(by: Int) {
        val actual = if (cargoWeight > 5000) by / 2 else by  // Heavy cargo = slow
        super.accelerate(actual)
        if (cargoWeight > 5000) println("⚠️ Heavy cargo — reduced acceleration!")
    }
}

fun main() {
    val tesla = ElectricCar("Tesla", "Model 3")
    tesla.accelerate(50)
    tesla.accelerate(30)
    tesla.brake()
    println(tesla.describe())
    
    val truck = Truck("Tata", "Prima")
    truck.cargoWeight = 8000
    truck.accelerate(40)
}
Multi-Level Inheritance:

kotlin
open class Shape {
    open fun area(): Double = 0.0
    open fun perimeter(): Double = 0.0
    fun describe() = "${this::class.simpleName}: Area=${area()}, Perimeter=${perimeter()}"
}

open class Polygon(val sides: Int) : Shape() {
    open val sideLength: Double = 0.0
}

class Rectangle(val width: Double, val height: Double) : Shape() {
    override fun area() = width * height
    override fun perimeter() = 2 * (width + height)
}

class Square(val side: Double) : Rectangle(side, side) {  // Square is Rectangle!
    override fun area() = side * side
    override fun perimeter() = 4 * side
}

class Circle(val radius: Double) : Shape() {
    override fun area() = Math.PI * radius * radius
    override fun perimeter() = 2 * Math.PI * radius  // circumference
}

fun main() {
    val shapes: List<Shape> = listOf(
        Rectangle(4.0, 6.0),
        Square(5.0),
        Circle(3.0)
    )
    shapes.forEach { println(it.describe()) }
}
Real-World Android Example:

kotlin
// Android Fragment hierarchy simulate karna
open class BaseFragment(val fragmentName: String) {
    protected var isVisible: Boolean = false
    protected val tag: String = fragmentName.uppercase()
    
    open fun onCreate() {
        println("[$tag] onCreate() called")
    }
    
    open fun onResume() {
        isVisible = true
        println("[$tag] onResume() — Fragment visible")
    }
    
    open fun onPause() {
        isVisible = false
        println("[$tag] onPause() — Fragment hidden")
    }
    
    fun onDestroy() {
        println("[$tag] onDestroy() — Fragment destroyed")
    }
}

class HomeFragment : BaseFragment("HomeFragment") {
    private val posts = mutableListOf<String>()
    
    override fun onCreate() {
        super.onCreate()  // ← Always call super!
        println("[$tag] Loading home feed...")
        posts.addAll(listOf("Post 1", "Post 2", "Post 3"))
    }
    
    override fun onResume() {
        super.onResume()
        println("[$tag] Refreshing ${posts.size} posts")
    }
    
    fun loadMorePosts() = println("[$tag] Loading more posts...")
}

class ProfileFragment(val userId: Int) : BaseFragment("ProfileFragment") {
    
    override fun onCreate() {
        super.onCreate()
        println("[$tag] Loading profile for user $userId...")
    }
    
    fun editProfile() = println("[$tag] Opening edit profile...")
}

fun main() {
    val home = HomeFragment()
    home.onCreate()
    home.onResume()
    home.onPause()
    
    val profile = ProfileFragment(42)
    profile.onCreate()
    profile.editProfile()
}
Real-Life Analogy
👨‍👩‍👧 Inheritance = Biological Inheritance

Tum apne parents se kuch cheezein inherit karte ho — aankhon ka color, height, nose shape (properties). Lekin tum apne khud ke skills bhi add karte ho — coding, cooking (methods). Yahi hai OOP inheritance! super.onCreate() = "Pehle papa wala kaam karo, phir apna karo"

Common Mistakes
kotlin
// ❌ MISTAKE 1: open keyword bhool gaya
class Animal { fun eat() = println("Eating") }
// class Dog : Animal() { }  // ERROR! Animal is final by default

// ✅
open class Animal { open fun eat() = println("Eating") }

// ❌ MISTAKE 2: Constructor chaining bhool gaya
open class Person(val name: String)
// class Student : Person { }  // ERROR! Must call Person constructor

// ✅
class Student(name: String, val grade: Int) : Person(name)

// ❌ MISTAKE 3: Super call bhool gaya (when needed)
open class Base {
    open fun doWork() {
        println("Base doing important initialization work")
    }
}
class Derived : Base() {
    override fun doWork() {
        // super.doWork()  // ❌ Forgot super — parent work not done!
        println("Derived work only")
    }
}

// ✅ Always consider if super should be called
Best Practices
Composition over Inheritance — Jab possible ho, interface ya composition prefer karo
super.method() call karo jab parent ka kaam important ho
Inheritance ki depth 2-3 levels tak rakho — zyada deep = confusing
Liskov Substitution Principle — Child class parent ki jagah use ho sake
override keyword clearly likho — compiler enforce karta hai
Interview Questions
Basic:

Q: Kotlin mein multiple inheritance kyu nahi hai?
A: Diamond problem ki wajah se — agar A aur B dono ka fun() ho, aur C dono se inherit kare, toh C.fun() kaun sa use kare? Solution: Interfaces use karo (multiple interfaces allowed, single class inheritance).
Advanced:

Q: Kotlin classes default final kyun hain?
A: Effective Java principle — "Design for inheritance or prohibit it." Unintended inheritance se bugs aate hain. open explicitly likhne se conscious decision hoti hai.
7. 🎭 POLYMORPHISM
What is it?
Polymorphism = "Poly" (many) + "Morph" (forms) = Ek cheez ke anek roop

Ek method ka naam same, behavior alag alag — caller ko tension nahi ki konsi exact implementation hai.

sound() method:
- Dog.sound()  → "Woof!"
- Cat.sound()  → "Meow!"
- Bird.sound() → "Tweet!"

Caller: animal.sound() — bina jaane ki woh Dog/Cat/Bird hai
Two Types:
Compile-Time Polymorphism (Static Dispatch) = Method Overloading — same naam, alag parameters

Runtime Polymorphism (Dynamic Dispatch) = Method Overriding — parent reference, child implementation

How it Works Internally
Runtime Polymorphism mein JVM vtable (virtual method table) use karta hai:

Har object ke saath ek hidden pointer hota hai vtable ki taraf
Vtable mein overridden methods ki actual implementation pointers hote hain
animal.sound() call hota hai → vtable se actual Dog.sound() pointer milta hai → Dog.sound() execute
Animal vtable:        Dog vtable:
┌──────────┐          ┌──────────┐
│ sound()  │──────▶   │ sound()  │──▶ Dog's sound implementation
│ eat()    │          │ eat()    │──▶ Animal's eat (not overridden)
└──────────┘          └──────────┘
Examples
Compile-Time Polymorphism (Overloading):

kotlin
class Calculator {
    // Same name, different parameters
    fun add(a: Int, b: Int): Int = a + b
    fun add(a: Double, b: Double): Double = a + b
    fun add(a: Int, b: Int, c: Int): Int = a + b + c
    fun add(a: String, b: String): String = a + b  // String concat
    
    fun format(value: Int) = "Integer: $value"
    fun format(value: Double) = "Double: ${"%.2f".format(value)}"
    fun format(value: String) = "String: $value"
}

fun main() {
    val calc = Calculator()
    println(calc.add(5, 3))           // 8 (Int version)
    println(calc.add(5.5, 3.2))       // 8.7 (Double version)
    println(calc.add(1, 2, 3))        // 6 (3-param version)
    println(calc.add("Hello", " World")) // Hello World (String)
    println(calc.format(42))          // Integer: 42
    println(calc.format(3.14))        // Double: 3.14
}
Runtime Polymorphism (Overriding):

kotlin
open class Notification(val title: String, val message: String) {
    open fun send() {
        println("📩 Sending notification: $title — $message")
    }
    
    open fun getIcon(): String = "🔔"
    
    fun display() {
        println("${getIcon()} $title: $message")
    }
}

class EmailNotification(
    title: String,
    message: String,
    private val recipient: String
) : Notification(title, message) {
    override fun send() {
        println("📧 Email to $recipient: [$title] $message")
    }
    override fun getIcon() = "📧"
}

class SMSNotification(
    title: String,
    message: String,
    private val phoneNumber: String
) : Notification(title, message) {
    override fun send() {
        println("📱 SMS to $phoneNumber: $message")
    }
    override fun getIcon() = "📱"
}

class PushNotification(
    title: String,
    message: String,
    private val deviceToken: String
) : Notification(title, message) {
    override fun send() {
        println("🔔 Push to device[$deviceToken]: $title — $message")
    }
    override fun getIcon() = "🔔"
}

// Polymorphic function — doesn't care which type!
fun sendAll(notifications: List<Notification>) {
    println("=== Sending ${notifications.size} notifications ===")
    notifications.forEach { notification ->
        notification.send()  // Runtime decides which send() to call!
        notification.display()
    }
}

fun main() {
    val notifications: List<Notification> = listOf(  // Parent type reference!
        EmailNotification("Welcome!", "Thank you for joining", "user@email.com"),
        SMSNotification("OTP", "Your OTP is 123456", "+91-9876543210"),
        PushNotification("Sale Alert", "50% off today!", "device_token_xyz"),
        EmailNotification("Invoice", "Your invoice is ready", "user@email.com")
    )
    
    sendAll(notifications)  // Polymorphism magic!
}
Real-World Android — Payment Polymorphism:

kotlin
abstract class PaymentMethod(
    val name: String,
    protected var amount: Double
) {
    abstract fun processPayment(): Boolean
    abstract fun getReceiptDetails(): String
    
    open fun validateAmount(): Boolean {
        return amount > 0
    }
    
    fun pay(): Boolean {
        println("💳 Processing ₹$amount via $name...")
        if (!validateAmount()) {
            println("❌ Invalid amount!")
            return false
        }
        val success = processPayment()
        if (success) {
            println("✅ Payment successful!")
            println(getReceiptDetails())
        }
        return success
    }
}

class UPIPayment(amount: Double, private val upiId: String) : PaymentMethod("UPI", amount) {
    override fun processPayment(): Boolean {
        println("  Sending to UPI ID: $upiId")
        return upiId.contains("@")  // Basic validation
    }
    override fun getReceiptDetails() = "UPI Ref: UPI${System.currentTimeMillis()}"
}

class CreditCardPayment(
    amount: Double,
    private val cardNumber: String,
    private val cvv: String
) : PaymentMethod("Credit Card", amount) {
    override fun processPayment(): Boolean {
        println("  Processing card: xxxx-xxxx-xxxx-${cardNumber.takeLast(4)}")
        return cardNumber.length == 16 && cvv.length == 3
    }
    override fun validateAmount() = amount > 0 && amount <= 100000.0  // Card limit
    override fun getReceiptDetails() = "Card Auth: AUTH${(1000..9999).random()}"
}

class WalletPayment(amount: Double, private var walletBalance: Double) : PaymentMethod("Wallet", amount) {
    override fun processPayment(): Boolean {
        if (amount > walletBalance) {
            println("  ❌ Insufficient wallet balance! Balance: ₹$walletBalance")
            return false
        }
        walletBalance -= amount
        println("  Wallet balance remaining: ₹$walletBalance")
        return true
    }
    override fun getReceiptDetails() = "Wallet TXN: WLT${System.currentTimeMillis()}"
}

fun checkout(payment: PaymentMethod) {  // Polymorphic — takes any payment!
    payment.pay()
}

fun main() {
    val payments: List<PaymentMethod> = listOf(
        UPIPayment(500.0, "rahul@okaxis"),
        CreditCardPayment(15000.0, "1234567890123456", "123"),
        WalletPayment(200.0, 150.0),  // Insufficient balance case
        WalletPayment(100.0, 500.0)
    )
    
    payments.forEach { payment ->
        println("\n--- ${payment.name} Payment ---")
        checkout(payment)  // Same function, different behavior!
    }
}
Real-Life Analogy
🎮 Remote Control = Polymorphism

Teri TV remote ka "Volume Up" button:

Sony TV mein → Sony ka volume system chalata hai
Samsung mein → Samsung ka volume system
LG mein → LG ka volume system
Button same hai, behavior alag! Yahi hai runtime polymorphism.

Interview Questions
Tricky:

Q: Method overloading compile-time kyun hai?
A: Because at compile time, compiler arguments ke types se decide kar leta hai konsa method call hoga. No runtime decision needed.
Q: Kotlin mein Any.equals() ka example do polymorphism ka?
A: equals() har class mein override hota hai differently — String.equals() characters compare karta hai, Int.equals() values compare karta hai, custom classes apna equals() define karte hain.
8. 🎭 ABSTRACTION
What is it?
Abstraction = "Kya karta hai" dikhao, "Kaise karta hai" chupaao.

Car chalate waqt:
✅ Tum jaante ho: Steering ghuma → car mudti hai
❌ Tum nahi jaante: Steering column, rack-pinion mechanism, hydraulic fluid

Steering wheel = Abstract Interface
Internal mechanism = Hidden Implementation
Why do we use it?
Complexity reduce karo — Users ko sirf relevant info chahiye
Maintainability — Implementation change karo bina interface todhe
Security — Dangerous internals expose mat karo
Team work — Team members parallel kaam kar sakti hain interfaces define karke
Abstraction Achieve Karna:
Abstract Classes — Partial abstraction
Interfaces — Full abstraction
9. 🏛️ ABSTRACT CLASSES
What is it?
Abstract class ek incomplete class hai jise directly instantiate nahi kar sakte. Isko inherit karna padta hai aur abstract methods implement karne padte hain.

Abstract class = Incomplete blueprint
Concrete class = Complete implementation
Syntax in Kotlin
kotlin
abstract class Shape {
    // Abstract property — must be overridden
    abstract val color: String
    
    // Abstract method — no body, must be overridden
    abstract fun area(): Double
    abstract fun perimeter(): Double
    
    // Concrete method — has body, inherited as-is
    fun describe() {
        println("${this::class.simpleName}: color=$color, area=${area()}, perimeter=${perimeter()}")
    }
    
    // Concrete method that calls abstract — Template Method Pattern!
    fun draw() {
        println("Drawing $color shape")
        setupCanvas()
        render()
        applyColor()
    }
    
    private fun setupCanvas() = println("Canvas setup done")
    protected abstract fun render()
    private fun applyColor() = println("Applying color: $color")
}

class Circle(override val color: String, val radius: Double) : Shape() {
    override fun area() = Math.PI * radius * radius
    override fun perimeter() = 2 * Math.PI * radius
    override fun render() = println("Rendering circle with radius $radius")
}

class Rectangle(override val color: String, val w: Double, val h: Double) : Shape() {
    override fun area() = w * h
    override fun perimeter() = 2 * (w + h)
    override fun render() = println("Rendering rectangle ${w}x${h}")
}
Examples
Real-World Example — Repository Pattern:

kotlin
// Abstract Repository — Database ka abstraction
abstract class BaseRepository<T, ID> {
    protected val cache = mutableMapOf<ID, T>()
    
    // Abstract — subclass decide karega kaise implement karna
    abstract fun findById(id: ID): T?
    abstract fun save(entity: T): Boolean
    abstract fun delete(id: ID): Boolean
    abstract fun findAll(): List<T>
    
    // Concrete — same for all
    fun existsById(id: ID): Boolean {
        return findById(id) != null
    }
    
    fun count(): Int = findAll().size
    
    fun saveAll(entities: List<T>): Int {
        return entities.count { save(it) }
    }
}

data class User(val id: Int, val name: String, val email: String)

class UserRepository : BaseRepository<User, Int>() {
    
    override fun findById(id: Int): User? {
        // Real app mein: database query
        return cache[id]
    }
    
    override fun save(entity: User): Boolean {
        return try {
            // Validation
            require(entity.name.isNotBlank()) { "Name required" }
            require(entity.email.contains("@")) { "Valid email required" }
            
            cache[entity.id] = entity
            println("✅ User saved: ${entity.name}")
            true
        } catch (e: IllegalArgumentException) {
            println("❌ Save failed: ${e.message}")
            false
        }
    }
    
    override fun delete(id: Int): Boolean {
        return if (cache.containsKey(id)) {
            cache.remove(id)
            println("✅ User $id deleted")
            true
        } else {
            println("❌ User $id not found")
            false
        }
    }
    
    override fun findAll(): List<User> = cache.values.toList()
    
    // Extra user-specific method
    fun findByEmail(email: String): User? {
        return cache.values.find { it.email == email }
    }
}

fun main() {
    val repo = UserRepository()
    repo.save(User(1, "Rahul", "rahul@test.com"))
    repo.save(User(2, "Priya", "priya@test.com"))
    repo.save(User(3, "", "invalid"))  // Will fail validation
    
    println("Total users: ${repo.count()}")
    println("User 1 exists: ${repo.existsById(1)}")
    println("Find by email: ${repo.findByEmail("priya@test.com")}")
    
    repo.delete(1)
    println("All users: ${repo.findAll()}")
}
Abstract vs Concrete — When to Use Which?
Use Abstract Class when:
✅ Common base implementation share karni ho
✅ Template Method Pattern chahiye (skeleton define karo)
✅ Constructor chahiye abstract type mein
✅ State (properties) abstract type mein share karni ho

Use Concrete Class when:
✅ Complete implementation hai
✅ Directly instantiate karna ho
Interview Questions
Advanced:

Q: Abstract class ka constructor ho sakta hai? Agar haan toh kaisa use karta hai?
A: Haan! Abstract class ka constructor ho sakta hai — lekin directly nahi call hota. Jab subclass instantiated hoti hai, uska constructor super() through abstract class ka constructor call karta hai. Initialization kaam ke liye useful hai.
10. 📋 INTERFACES
What is it?
Interface ek contract hai — "Jo bhi mujhe implement karega, usse ye sab methods provide karne honge."

Interface = Job Description
Implementing Class = Employee who fulfills that job description

Interface: "Payment karna aana chahiye"
UPIPayment: "Haan! Mujhe UPI se payment karna aata hai"
CardPayment: "Haan! Mujhe Card se payment karna aata hai"
Key Differences from Abstract Class:
Feature	Abstract Class	Interface
State (properties)	Yes	Limited (no backing field by default)
Constructor	Yes	No
Multiple inheritance	No	Yes (multiple interfaces)
Access modifiers	All	public by default
Default implementation	Yes (from Kotlin)	Yes (from Kotlin)
Syntax in Kotlin
kotlin
interface Drawable {
    val color: String  // Abstract property — no backing field
    
    fun draw()  // Abstract method
    
    fun clear() {  // Default implementation (Kotlin feature!)
        println("Clearing $color drawing")
    }
}

interface Resizable {
    fun resize(factor: Double)
    fun getSize(): Pair<Double, Double>
}

// Multiple interface implementation!
class Canvas(override val color: String) : Drawable, Resizable {
    private var width = 100.0
    private var height = 100.0
    
    override fun draw() = println("Drawing on $color canvas (${width}x${height})")
    override fun resize(factor: Double) {
        width *= factor
        height *= factor
        println("Resized to ${width}x${height}")
    }
    override fun getSize() = Pair(width, height)
}
Examples
Comprehensive Interface Example:

kotlin
interface Authenticatable {
    fun login(username: String, password: String): Boolean
    fun logout()
    fun isLoggedIn(): Boolean
    
    // Default implementation
    fun getSessionInfo(): String = "Session active: ${isLoggedIn()}"
}

interface Auditable {
    val createdAt: Long
    val createdBy: String
    var updatedAt: Long
    
    fun getAuditLog(): String = "Created: $createdAt by $createdBy | Updated: $updatedAt"
}

interface Serializable {
    fun toJson(): String
    fun fromJson(json: String): Any
}

// Class implementing multiple interfaces
class UserService(
    override val createdBy: String
) : Authenticatable, Auditable {
    
    override val createdAt: Long = System.currentTimeMillis()
    override var updatedAt: Long = createdAt
    
    private var _isLoggedIn = false
    private var currentUser: String? = null
    private val validUsers = mapOf("rahul" to "pass123", "priya" to "pass456")
    
    override fun login(username: String, password: String): Boolean {
        return if (validUsers[username] == password) {
            _isLoggedIn = true
            currentUser = username
            updatedAt = System.currentTimeMillis()
            println("✅ $username logged in successfully")
            true
        } else {
            println("❌ Invalid credentials for $username")
            false
        }
    }
    
    override fun logout() {
        println("👋 $currentUser logged out")
        _isLoggedIn = false
        currentUser = null
        updatedAt = System.currentTimeMillis()
    }
    
    override fun isLoggedIn() = _isLoggedIn
    
    fun getCurrentUser() = currentUser
}

fun main() {
    val service = UserService("System")
    
    println(service.getAuditLog())
    
    service.login("rahul", "wrongpass")  // ❌
    service.login("rahul", "pass123")    // ✅
    
    println(service.getSessionInfo())  // Default implementation
    println("Current user: ${service.getCurrentUser()}")
    
    service.logout()
    println(service.getSessionInfo())
}
Real-World Android-style Interface:

kotlin
// Callback interfaces — Android mein bohot common
interface OnItemClickListener {
    fun onItemClick(position: Int, itemId: Long)
    fun onItemLongClick(position: Int): Boolean = false  // Default = no action
}

interface NetworkCallback {
    fun onSuccess(response: String)
    fun onError(error: String)
    fun onLoading(isLoading: Boolean) { }  // Default empty
}

interface LifecycleOwner {
    fun onStart()
    fun onStop()
    fun onDestroy()
}

// Simulating an Android Activity
class MainActivity : LifecycleOwner, OnItemClickListener {
    private val items = listOf("Product A", "Product B", "Product C")
    
    override fun onStart() {
        println("🟢 MainActivity started")
        loadData()
    }
    
    override fun onStop() = println("🟡 MainActivity stopped")
    override fun onDestroy() = println("🔴 MainActivity destroyed")
    
    override fun onItemClick(position: Int, itemId: Long) {
        println("Clicked: ${items[position]} (id: $itemId)")
        navigateToDetail(itemId)
    }
    
    override fun onItemLongClick(position: Int): Boolean {
        println("Long clicked: ${items[position]} — showing options menu")
        return true  // Event consumed
    }
    
    private fun loadData() {
        val networkCallback = object : NetworkCallback {
            override fun onSuccess(response: String) {
                println("✅ Data loaded: $response")
            }
            override fun onError(error: String) {
                println("❌ Error: $error")
            }
            override fun onLoading(isLoading: Boolean) {
                println("Loading: $isLoading")
            }
        }
        
        networkCallback.onLoading(true)
        networkCallback.onSuccess("3 products fetched")
        networkCallback.onLoading(false)
    }
    
    private fun navigateToDetail(id: Long) = println("Navigating to detail: $id")
}

fun main() {
    val activity = MainActivity()
    activity.onStart()
    activity.onItemClick(1, 102L)
    activity.onItemLongClick(0)
    activity.onStop()
    activity.onDestroy()
}
Real-Life Analogy
📄 Interface = Electrical Socket Standard

India mein 3-pin socket standard hai — ye ek interface hai.
Koi bhi company fan, AC, fridge bana sakti hai — bas unhe 3-pin plug (implement interface) lagana hai.
Socket ko nahi pata ki fan hai ya AC — woh sirf standard follow karta hai.

Common Mistakes
kotlin
// ❌ MISTAKE 1: Interface mein state rakhna
interface Wrong {
    var count = 0  // ERROR! Interface properties can't have initializers (unless companion)
}

// ✅ CORRECT — Abstract property
interface Correct {
    var count: Int  // Abstract — implementing class provides state
}

// ❌ MISTAKE 2: Interface instance banana
// val x = Drawable()  // ERROR!

// ✅ CORRECT: Implement karo ya anonymous object
val x = object : Drawable {
    override val color = "Blue"
    override fun draw() = println("Drawing")
}

// ❌ MISTAKE 3: Diamond problem ignore karna
interface A { fun hello() = println("A") }
interface B { fun hello() = println("B") }

class C : A, B {
    // override fun hello() = ???  // MUST override when ambiguous!
    override fun hello() {
        super<A>.hello()  // Specify which one!
    }
}
Interview Questions
Advanced:

Q: Interface mein companion object kab use karte hain?
A: Factory methods define karne ke liye, ya interface-level constants ke liye:
kotlin
interface Config {
    companion object {
        const val MAX_RETRY = 3
        const val TIMEOUT = 30000L
        fun create(): Config = DefaultConfig()  // Factory
    }
}
🔷 SECTION 3: ADVANCED OOP IN KOTLIN
1. 📊 DATA CLASSES
What is it?
Data class ek special class hai jo primarily data hold karne ke liye hoti hai. Kotlin automatically equals(), hashCode(), toString(), copy(), aur componentN() functions generate karta hai.

Why use it?
kotlin
// Normal class — 40+ lines likhne padte hain:
class User(val name: String, val email: String) {
    override fun equals(other: Any?): Boolean { /* boilerplate */ }
    override fun hashCode(): Int { /* boilerplate */ }
    override fun toString(): String { /* boilerplate */ }
    fun copy(name: String = this.name, email: String = this.email) = User(name, email)
}

// Data class — 1 line!
data class User(val name: String, val email: String)
// Kotlin generates ALL of the above automatically!
Syntax & Features:
kotlin
data class Product(
    val id: Int,
    val name: String,
    val price: Double,
    val category: String = "General"  // Default values allowed!
) {
    // Extra properties NOT in constructor (not part of equals/hashCode)
    val formattedPrice: String
        get() = "₹$price"
    
    // Extra methods allowed!
    fun applyDiscount(percent: Double) = copy(price = price * (1 - percent / 100))
}

fun main() {
    val p1 = Product(1, "Phone", 15000.0, "Electronics")
    val p2 = Product(1, "Phone", 15000.0, "Electronics")
    val p3 = Product(2, "Laptop", 55000.0)
    
    // toString() — auto generated!
    println(p1)  // Product(id=1, name=Phone, price=15000.0, category=Electronics)
    
    // equals() — structural equality!
    println(p1 == p2)  // true (same data)
    println(p1 == p3)  // false
    
    // copy() — immutable update pattern!
    val updated = p1.copy(price = 12000.0)
    println(updated)  // Product(id=1, name=Phone, price=12000.0, category=Electronics)
    
    // Discount apply
    val discounted = p1.applyDiscount(10.0)
    println(discounted)  // price = 13500.0
    
    // Destructuring (componentN functions)
    val (id, name, price, category) = p1
    println("$id: $name costs ₹$price in $category")
    
    // In a Set (uses hashCode)
    val products = setOf(p1, p2, p3)  // p1 and p2 are same, so only 2 in set
    println("Unique products: ${products.size}")  // 2
}
Real-World API Response Model:

kotlin
data class ApiResponse<T>(
    val success: Boolean,
    val data: T? = null,
    val error: String? = null,
    val statusCode: Int = 200
) {
    val isSuccess: Boolean get() = success && error == null
}

data class LoginRequest(
    val username: String,
    val password: String,
    val rememberMe: Boolean = false
)

data class UserDTO(
    val id: Long,
    val username: String,
    val email: String,
    val role: String,
    val token: String
)

fun simulateLogin(request: LoginRequest): ApiResponse<UserDTO> {
    return if (request.username == "admin" && request.password == "admin123") {
        ApiResponse(
            success = true,
            data = UserDTO(1L, "admin", "admin@app.com", "ADMIN", "jwt_token_xyz"),
            statusCode = 200
        )
    } else {
        ApiResponse(
            success = false,
            error = "Invalid credentials",
            statusCode = 401
        )
    }
}

fun main() {
    val request = LoginRequest("admin", "admin123", true)
    val response = simulateLogin(request)
    
    if (response.isSuccess) {
        val user = response.data!!
        println("Welcome ${user.username}! Token: ${user.token}")
    } else {
        println("Error ${response.statusCode}: ${response.error}")
    }
    
    // Copy for testing
    val wrongRequest = request.copy(password = "wrongpass")
    val failResponse = simulateLogin(wrongRequest)
    println("Failed login: ${failResponse.error}")
}
2. 🔒 SEALED CLASSES
What is it?
Sealed class ek restricted class hierarchy hai — uske subclasses limited aur defined hoti hain. Compiler ko pata hota hai ki sealed class ke exactly kaun kaun se subtypes hain.

Sealed Class = "Closed world" — sab possibilities known hain
Like traffic light: only RED, YELLOW, GREEN — no other option
Why use it?
when expression ke saath perfect — else branch ki zarurat nahi kyunki compiler jaanta hai sab cases!

Syntax:
kotlin
sealed class Result<out T> {
    data class Success<T>(val data: T) : Result<T>()
    data class Error(val message: String, val code: Int = -1) : Result<Nothing>()
    object Loading : Result<Nothing>()
    object Empty : Result<Nothing>()
}

// Usage — EXHAUSTIVE when (no else needed!)
fun handleResult(result: Result<String>) = when (result) {
    is Result.Success -> println("✅ Data: ${result.data}")
    is Result.Error -> println("❌ Error ${result.code}: ${result.message}")
    is Result.Loading -> println("⏳ Loading...")
    is Result.Empty -> println("📭 No data")
    // No else needed — all cases covered!
}
Real-World Example — UI States:

kotlin
sealed class UiState<out T> {
    object Idle : UiState<Nothing>()
    object Loading : UiState<Nothing>()
    data class Success<T>(val data: T) : UiState<T>()
    data class Error(
        val message: String,
        val throwable: Throwable? = null,
        val isRetryable: Boolean = true
    ) : UiState<Nothing>()
}

sealed class NavigationEvent {
    object Back : NavigationEvent()
    data class ToDetail(val id: Long) : NavigationEvent()
    data class ToProfile(val userId: Int) : NavigationEvent()
    data class ShowDialog(val title: String, val message: String) : NavigationEvent()
    object ToLogin : NavigationEvent()
}

class ProductViewModel {
    private var uiState: UiState<List<String>> = UiState.Idle
    
    fun loadProducts() {
        uiState = UiState.Loading
        renderUi()
        
        // Simulate network delay
        Thread.sleep(100)
        
        // Simulate success
        uiState = UiState.Success(listOf("Phone", "Laptop", "Tablet"))
        renderUi()
    }
    
    fun loadFailed() {
        uiState = UiState.Error("Network error", isRetryable = true)
        renderUi()
    }
    
    private fun renderUi() = when (val state = uiState) {
        is UiState.Idle -> println("🔵 Idle state")
        is UiState.Loading -> println("⏳ Showing loading spinner...")
        is UiState.Success -> println("✅ Showing ${state.data.size} products: ${state.data}")
        is UiState.Error -> {
            println("❌ Error: ${state.message}")
            if (state.isRetryable) println("  [Retry] button shown")
        }
    }
}

fun handleNavigation(event: NavigationEvent) = when (event) {
    is NavigationEvent.Back -> println("⬅️ Going back")
    is NavigationEvent.ToDetail -> println("📱 Opening detail: ${event.id}")
    is NavigationEvent.ToProfile -> println("👤 Opening profile: ${event.userId}")
    is NavigationEvent.ShowDialog -> println("💬 Dialog: ${event.title} — ${event.message}")
    is NavigationEvent.ToLogin -> println("🔐 Going to login")
}

fun main() {
    val vm = ProductViewModel()
    vm.loadProducts()
    vm.loadFailed()
    
    handleNavigation(NavigationEvent.ToDetail(42L))
    handleNavigation(NavigationEvent.ShowDialog("Error", "Please try again"))
}
3. 🎨 ENUM CLASSES
What is it?
Enum class ek fixed set of constants define karta hai. Jab possible values limited aur known hain.

kotlin
enum class Direction { NORTH, SOUTH, EAST, WEST }
enum class DayOfWeek { MON, TUE, WED, THU, FRI, SAT, SUN }
Advanced Enum with Properties & Methods:
kotlin
enum class HttpStatus(val code: Int, val description: String) {
    OK(200, "Success"),
    CREATED(201, "Created"),
    BAD_REQUEST(400, "Bad Request"),
    UNAUTHORIZED(401, "Unauthorized"),
    NOT_FOUND(404, "Not Found"),
    INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR(500, "Internal Server Error");
    
    val isSuccess: Boolean get() = code in 200..299
    val isClientError: Boolean get() = code in 400..499
    val isServerError: Boolean get() = code in 500..599
    
    fun toResponse(): String = "$code $description"
    
    companion object {
        fun fromCode(code: Int): HttpStatus? = values().find { it.code == code }
    }
}

enum class Priority(val level: Int, val label: String) {
    LOW(1, "🟢 Low"),
    MEDIUM(5, "🟡 Medium"),
    HIGH(8, "🔴 High"),
    CRITICAL(10, "🚨 Critical");
    
    fun isUrgent() = level >= HIGH.level
    
    companion object {
        fun fromLevel(level: Int) = values().minByOrNull { Math.abs(it.level - level) }!!
    }
}

fun main() {
    // Basic usage
    val status = HttpStatus.NOT_FOUND
    println(status.toResponse())      // 404 Not Found
    println(status.isClientError)     // true
    
    // Find by code
    val found = HttpStatus.fromCode(500)
    println(found?.description)       // Internal Server Error
    
    // Enum in when
    val response = HttpStatus.OK
    val message = when (response) {
        HttpStatus.OK, HttpStatus.CREATED -> "Request successful!"
        HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST -> "Check your input"
        HttpStatus.UNAUTHORIZED -> "Please login"
        HttpStatus.NOT_FOUND -> "Resource not found"
        HttpStatus.INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR -> "Server error, try later"
    }
    println(message)
    
    // Enum properties
    println(Priority.CRITICAL.isUrgent())  // true
    println(Priority.LOW.isUrgent())       // false
    
    // Iteration
    Priority.values().forEach { p ->
        println("${p.label}: Level ${p.level} | Urgent: ${p.isUrgent()}")
    }
}
4. 🤝 COMPANION OBJECTS
What is it?
Companion Object ek singleton object hai jo kisi class ke saath associated hota hai. Java ke static members ki tarah kaam karta hai.

kotlin
class MyClass {
    companion object {
        // Accessed as: MyClass.METHOD_NAME (like static)
        const val MAX_SIZE = 100
        fun create() = MyClass()
    }
}
Examples:
kotlin
class DatabaseConfig private constructor(
    val host: String,
    val port: Int,
    val database: String
) {
    companion object {
        private const val DEFAULT_HOST = "localhost"
        private const val DEFAULT_PORT = 5432
        
        // Factory methods
        fun development() = DatabaseConfig(DEFAULT_HOST, DEFAULT_PORT, "dev_db")
        fun production() = DatabaseConfig("prod.server.com", DEFAULT_PORT, "prod_db")
        fun test() = DatabaseConfig(DEFAULT_HOST, 5433, "test_db")
        
        // Utility
        fun fromUrl(url: String): DatabaseConfig {
            // Parse: "host:port/database"
            val parts = url.split(":", "/")
            return DatabaseConfig(parts[0], parts[1].toInt(), parts[2])
        }
    }
    
    override fun toString() = "DatabaseConfig($host:$port/$database)"
}

fun main() {
    val devDb = DatabaseConfig.development()  // Like static method!
    val prodDb = DatabaseConfig.production()
    val fromUrl = DatabaseConfig.fromUrl("myserver:3306/mydb")
    
    println(devDb)    // DatabaseConfig(localhost:5432/dev_db)
    println(prodDb)
    println(fromUrl)
    
    // Private constructor — can't do new DatabaseConfig(...)
    // Only factory methods work!
}
5. 🌐 OBJECT KEYWORD (SINGLETON)
What is it?
object keyword se ek singleton class create hoti hai — ek hi instance kabhi bhi. Automatically thread-safe in Kotlin.

kotlin
object AppConfig {
    const val APP_NAME = "MyApp"
    const val VERSION = "1.0.0"
    var debugMode = false
    
    fun getBaseUrl() = if (debugMode) "http://localhost:8080" else "https://api.myapp.com"
}

// Usage — no instantiation needed!
println(AppConfig.APP_NAME)
AppConfig.debugMode = true
println(AppConfig.getBaseUrl())

kotlin
// One-time use implementation
val clickListener = object : OnItemClickListener {
    override fun onItemClick(position: Int, itemId: Long) {
        println("Clicked position $position")
    }
