    (Controlled Access!)
    
Tum seedha andar haath nahi daal sakte.
Sirf ek taraf se paisa daalna allowed hai.
Paisa nikalna = Bank tod do (limited/controlled)
Encapsulation = Data ko chupaao + Controlled access do

🌍 Real-Life Example
ATM Machine = Perfect Encapsulation!

ATM ke andar:
┌───────────────────────────────────────┐
│  💰 Cash Storage          (PRIVATE)   │
│  🔐 Account Database      (PRIVATE)   │
│  ⚙️ Processing System     (PRIVATE)   │
│  🔢 PIN Verification      (PRIVATE)   │
└──────────────────────────────────────-┘
        ↑
   Tumhe andar haath nahi daalna
        ↓
┌───────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Card Slot        (PUBLIC interface)  │
│  PIN Pad          (PUBLIC interface)  │
│  Amount Buttons   (PUBLIC interface)  │
│  Cash Slot        (PUBLIC interface)  │
└───────────────────────────────────────┘

Tum sirf bahar ke buttons use karte ho.
Internal logic chupaaya hua hai.
🤔 Why Encapsulation Exist Karta Hai?
kotlin
// BINA ENCAPSULATION KE — Disaster! 🚨

class BankAccount {
    var balance = 10000.0  // Public! Koi bhi change kar sakta hai!
}

val acc = BankAccount()
acc.balance = 1000000000.0   // 😱 Koi bhi balance change kar le!
acc.balance = -5000.0         // 😱 Negative balance!
acc.balance = Double.MAX_VALUE // 😱 Kuch bhi kar sakte hain!

// KISI KO BHI ROKA NAHI JA SAKTA!
kotlin
// ENCAPSULATION KE SAATH — Safe! 🔒

class BankAccount {
    private var balance = 10000.0  // Private! Bahar se access nahi

    fun deposit(amount: Double) {
        if (amount > 0) balance += amount  // Validation!
        else println("Amount positive honi chahiye!")
    }

    fun getBalance() = balance  // Read-only access
}

val acc = BankAccount()
// acc.balance = 1000000.0  ← ❌ ERROR! Private hai
acc.deposit(-500.0)          // "Amount positive honi chahiye!"
acc.deposit(5000.0)          // ✅ Balance safe way se badhega
⚙️ Internally Kaise Kaam Karta Hai?
Visibility Modifiers (Darwaze) — 4 levels:

🌐 public   = Ghar ka main door
              Sab aa sakte hain (Default in Kotlin)

🔒 private  = Master bedroom ka lock
              Sirf isi class ke andar

👨‍👩‍👧 protected = Family room
              Class + uske children (subclasses)

🏢 internal = Office building
              Same module/project ke log
CLASS KA STRUCTURE:
┌────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              BankAccount                   │
│                                            │
│  🔒 PRIVATE ZONE (Gupt/Secret):            │
│     var balance: Double                    │
│     var pin: String                        │
│     fun validatePin(): Boolean             │
│                                            │
│  🌐 PUBLIC ZONE (Bahar diktha hai):        │
│     fun deposit(amount: Double)            │
│     fun withdraw(amount: Double, pin: String│
│     fun getBalance(): Double               │
└────────────────────────────────────────────┘
💻 Kotlin Syntax
kotlin
class Person(
    val name: String,                  // public (default) — koi bhi dekh sakta
    private val aadhaarNumber: String, // private — sirf class ke andar
    protected val age: Int,            // protected — class + children
    internal val department: String    // internal — same module
) {
    // Private property — custom getter/setter
    private var _salary: Double = 0.0
    
    // Public getter — read karo
    val salary: Double
        get() = _salary
    
    // Public setter with validation
    fun setSalary(amount: Double) {
        if (amount > 0) _salary = amount
        else println("❌ Invalid salary!")
    }
}
🧪 Code Examples
Easy Example
kotlin
class Student {
    // Private — sirf class ke andar change hoga
    private var _marks: Int = 0
    
    // Read-only access — Public
    val marks: Int
        get() = _marks
    
    // Controlled way to set marks
    fun setMarks(value: Int) {
        when {
            value < 0 -> println("❌ Marks negative nahi ho sakte!")
            value > 100 -> println("❌ Marks 100 se zyada nahi ho sakte!")
            else -> {
                _marks = value
                println("✅ Marks set: $_marks")
            }
        }
    }
    
    val grade: String
        get() = when {
            _marks >= 90 -> "A+"
            _marks >= 80 -> "A"
            _marks >= 70 -> "B"
            else -> "C"
        }
}

fun main() {
    val student = Student()
    
    // student._marks = 150  // ❌ ERROR! Private hai
    
    student.setMarks(150)   // ❌ "Marks 100 se zyada nahi ho sakte!"
    student.setMarks(-10)   // ❌ "Marks negative nahi ho sakte!"
    student.setMarks(85)    // ✅ "Marks set: 85"
    
    println("Marks: ${student.marks}")   // ✅ Read kar sakte hain
    println("Grade: ${student.grade}")   // A
}
Medium Example — Full Encapsulation
kotlin
class MobilePhone(
    private val imei: String,       // ← Kabhi change nahi hona chahiye
    val model: String
) {
    private var _batteryLevel: Int = 100
    private var _isLocked: Boolean = true
    private var _pin: String = "1234"
    private val _callLog = mutableListOf<String>()
    
    // Read-only properties
    val batteryLevel: Int get() = _batteryLevel
    val isLocked: Boolean get() = _isLocked
    val callLog: List<String> get() = _callLog.toList()  // Copy dete hain, original nahi!
    
    // Unlock karna
    fun unlock(pin: String): Boolean {
        return if (pin == _pin) {
            _isLocked = false
            println("🔓 Phone unlock ho gaya!")
            true
        } else {
            println("❌ Galat PIN!")
            false
        }
    }
    
    // Call karna — locked phone se call nahi hoga
    fun makeCall(number: String): Boolean {
        if (_isLocked) {
            println("❌ Phone lock hai! Pehle unlock karo.")
            return false
        }
        _callLog.add(number)
        _batteryLevel -= 5
        println("📞 Calling $number... Battery: $_batteryLevel%")
        return true
    }
    
    // PIN change
    fun changePin(oldPin: String, newPin: String): Boolean {
        if (oldPin != _pin) {
            println("❌ Purana PIN galat hai!")
            return false
        }
        if (newPin.length != 4) {
            println("❌ PIN 4 digits ka hona chahiye!")
            return false
        }
        _pin = newPin
        println("✅ PIN change ho gaya!")
        return true
    }
    
    fun charge(percentage: Int) {
        _batteryLevel = minOf(100, _batteryLevel + percentage)
        println("🔋 Battery: $_batteryLevel%")
    }
}

fun main() {
    val myPhone = MobilePhone("123456789012345", "Samsung S24")
    
    // _imei access nahi hoga — private hai
    // myPhone._pin = "0000"  ← ERROR!
    
    myPhone.makeCall("9876543210")      // ❌ Locked hai
    myPhone.unlock("9999")              // ❌ Galat PIN
    myPhone.unlock("1234")              // ✅ Unlock!
    myPhone.makeCall("9876543210")      // ✅ Call hogi
    myPhone.makeCall("1234567890")      // ✅ Call hogi
    
    println("Call log: ${myPhone.callLog}")
    // myPhone._callLog.clear()  ← ❌ ERROR! Private + Copy diya
    
    myPhone.changePin("1234", "5678")
}
Real-World Android Example
kotlin
// Android mein UserSession manage karna — Encapsulation ka best use
class UserSession {
    // Kisi ko bhi seedha access nahi
    private var _userId: Int = -1
    private var _token: String = ""
    private var _username: String = ""
    private var _isLoggedIn: Boolean = false
    private var _loginTime: Long = 0L
    
    // Read-only public access
    val userId: Int get() = _userId
    val username: String get() = _username
    val isLoggedIn: Boolean get() = _isLoggedIn
    
    // Session kitni der se active hai
    val sessionDuration: Long
        get() = if (_isLoggedIn) System.currentTimeMillis() - _loginTime else 0L
    
    fun login(userId: Int, username: String, token: String) {
        require(token.isNotBlank()) { "Token required!" }
        _userId = userId
        _username = username
        _token = token
        _isLoggedIn = true
        _loginTime = System.currentTimeMillis()
        println("✅ Session started: $username")
    }
    
    fun logout() {
        _userId = -1
        _token = ""
        _username = ""
        _isLoggedIn = false
        _loginTime = 0L
        println("👋 Session ended")
    }
    
    // Token sirf authorized jagah mile
    fun getAuthHeader(): String {
        return if (_isLoggedIn) "Bearer $_token"
        else throw IllegalStateException("Not logged in!")
    }
    
    companion object {
        // Singleton — ek hi session
        private var instance: UserSession? = null
        fun getInstance(): UserSession {
            if (instance == null) instance = UserSession()
            return instance!!
        }
    }
}

fun main() {
    val session = UserSession.getInstance()
    
    session.login(101, "rahul_dev", "jwt_abc_xyz_123")
    println("Logged in: ${session.isLoggedIn}")
    println("User: ${session.username}")
    println("Auth: ${session.getAuthHeader()}")
    
    // session._token  ← ❌ Private!
    
    session.logout()
    // session.getAuthHeader()  ← ❌ Exception! Not logged in
}
⚠️ Common Mistakes
kotlin
// ❌ GALTI 1: Sab kuch public rakhna
class User {
    var password: String = ""        // ❌ Anyone can read!
    var creditCardNumber: String = "" // ❌ Security disaster!
}

// ✅ SAHI
class User {
    private var password: String = ""
    private var creditCardNumber: String = ""
    
    fun login(inputPassword: String) = password == inputPassword
}

// ❌ GALTI 2: Mutable collection expose karna
class ShoppingCart {
    val items = mutableListOf<String>()  // ❌ Anyone can clear/modify!
}
val cart = ShoppingCart()
cart.items.clear()      // ❌ Seedha modify kar diya!
cart.items.add("Hack")  // ❌ Kuch bhi add kar diya!

// ✅ SAHI — Copy return karo
class ShoppingCart {
    private val _items = mutableListOf<String>()
    val items: List<String> get() = _items.toList()  // Immutable copy!
    
    fun addItem(item: String) { _items.add(item) }
    fun removeItem(item: String) { _items.remove(item) }
}

// ❌ GALTI 3: Getter/Setter banana instead of Kotlin properties
// Java style — Kotlin mein mat karo!
class Wrong {
    private var name: String = ""
    fun getName() = name
    fun setName(value: String) { name = value }
}

// ✅ Kotlin style — properties!
class Right {
    var name: String = ""
        private set  // Bahar se read-only, andar se change kar sakte hain
}
💡 Pro Tips
💡 TIP 1: "_" prefix private properties ke liye
   private var _items = mutableListOf()
   val items: List get() = _items.toList()

💡 TIP 2: private set use karo — read public, write private
   var score: Int = 0
       private set  ← Bahar se change nahi hoga!

💡 TIP 3: Immutable copy return karo collections ke liye
   get() = _list.toList()  ← Original safe!

💡 TIP 4: require() aur check() use karo validation ke liye
   require(amount > 0) { "Positive amount chahiye" }
🔥 Interview Questions
Q1: Encapsulation kya hai aur kyun zaroori hai?

"Encapsulation matlab hai data ko private rakhna aur
sirf controlled methods se access dena.

Kyun zaroori:
1. Data protection — koi bhi galat value set na kar sake
2. Validation — data set hone se pehle check kar sako
3. Flexibility — internal implementation change karo
   bina outside code todhe
4. Security — sensitive info hide karo

Real example: ATM mein aap seedha cash storage access
nahi karte — sirf interface (buttons) use karte ho."
Q2: private set ka kya matlab hai Kotlin mein?

var count: Int = 0
    private set

count ek public property hai — koi bhi read kar sakta hai.
Lekin set (write/change) sirf class ke andar ho sakta hai.

Bahar se: println(obj.count)  ← ✅ Read kar sakte
          obj.count = 5       ← ❌ Error! Private set
🧩 Practice Question
Sawaal: Ek Wallet class banao:

Private balance
addMoney() — sirf positive amount
spendMoney() — balance check karo pehle
getBalance() — read-only
kotlin
class Wallet(private var balance: Double = 0.0) {
    
    init {
        require(balance >= 0) { "Balance negative nahi ho sakta!" }
        println("👛 Wallet khola! Balance: ₹$balance")
    }
    
    fun addMoney(amount: Double) {
        if (amount <= 0) {
            println("❌ Amount positive honi chahiye!")
            return
        }
        balance += amount
        println("💰 ₹$amount add hua! Balance: ₹$balance")
    }
    
    fun spendMoney(amount: Double, description: String): Boolean {
        return when {
            amount <= 0 -> {
                println("❌ Amount positive honi chahiye!")
                false
            }
            amount > balance -> {
                println("❌ Paisa kam hai! Balance: ₹$balance, Chahiye: ₹$amount")
                false
            }
            else -> {
                balance -= amount
                println("✅ ₹$amount kharch: $description | Remaining: ₹$balance")
                true
            }
        }
    }
    
    fun getBalance(): Double = balance
}

fun main() {
    val myWallet = Wallet(500.0)
    myWallet.addMoney(1000.0)
    myWallet.spendMoney(200.0, "Chai aur Samosa ☕")
    myWallet.spendMoney(2000.0, "iPhone")  // ❌ Balance kam hai
    myWallet.spendMoney(-50.0, "Hack try")  // ❌ Negative amount
    println("Final balance: ₹${myWallet.getBalance()}")
}
🧠 Quick Summary + Memory Trick
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  QUICK SUMMARY                                       │
│  • Encapsulation = Data chupaao + Safe access do    │
│  • private = Class ke andar hi accessible           │
│  • Public methods = Controlled door/gate            │
│  • Custom getter/setter = Data pe control           │
│  • Immutable copies return karo collections ke liye │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

🧠 MEMORY TRICK:
"Encapsulation = CAPSULE (medicine)"
Capsule ke andar medicine chupi hai.
Tum seedha medicine nahi khaate —
capsule ke through khaate ho (controlled!)
🟦 LEVEL 2: THE 4 PILLARS
🔹 CONCEPT 4: INHERITANCE
🧒 Simple Explanation
Socho Papa se tumhe kya mila (inheritance!):

PAPA (Parent):
✅ Aankhon ka color — Tumhe mila!
✅ Height — Tumhe mila!
✅ Nose shape — Tumhe mila!
✅ Kuch habits — Tumhe mili!

TUM (Child):
✅ Papa wali sab cheezein (inherited)
✅ Plus apni nai cheezein (extra skills, personality)
Programming mein:

Parent Class = Papa ki properties aur methods
Child Class = Papa ki cheezein + apni nai cheezein
🌍 Real-Life Example
School mein departments socho:

Employee (Parent Class)
├── name
├── salary
├── workHours()
├── getPayslip()
│
├── Teacher (Child Class)
│   └── + subject, classRoom
│   └── + teachStudents(), gradePapers()
│
├── Principal (Child Class)
│   └── + schoolBudget, yearsOfExperience
│   └── + attendMeetings(), makeDecisions()
│
└── Librarian (Child Class)
    └── + totalBooks, sections
    └── + issueBook(), collectBook()
Sab employees hain — sab ke paas naam, salary hai.
Lekin har ek ka extra kaam alag hai!

🤔 Why Inheritance Exist Karta Hai?
Problem without inheritance:

kotlin
class Dog {
    var name = ""
    fun eat() = println("$name khaana kha raha hai")  // Same!
    fun sleep() = println("$name so raha hai")          // Same!
    fun bark() = println("$name: Woof!")                // Dog ka apna
}

class Cat {
    var name = ""
    fun eat() = println("$name khaana kha raha hai")  // COPY-PASTE! 😫
    fun sleep() = println("$name so raha hai")          // COPY-PASTE! 😫
    fun meow() = println("$name: Meow!")                // Cat ka apna
}

class Bird {
    var name = ""
    fun eat() = println("$name khaana kha raha hai")  // COPY-PASTE AGAIN! 😩
    fun sleep() = println("$name so raha hai")          // COPY-PASTE AGAIN! 😩
    fun fly() = println("$name ud raha hai")            // Bird ka apna
}
With inheritance:

kotlin
open class Animal(val name: String) {
    fun eat() = println("$name khaana kha raha hai")  // ← SIRF EK BAAR!
    fun sleep() = println("$name so raha hai")          // ← SIRF EK BAAR!
}

class Dog(name: String) : Animal(name) { fun bark() = println("$name: Woof!") }
class Cat(name: String) : Animal(name) { fun meow() = println("$name: Meow!") }
class Bird(name: String) : Animal(name) { fun fly() = println("$name ud raha hai") }
⚙️ Internally Kaise Kaam Karta Hai?
Dog object ke andar actually yeh hota hai:
┌────────────────────────────────────┐
│           Dog Object               │
│                                    │
│  📦 Animal part (inherited):       │
│     name: String                   │
│     fun eat()                      │
│     fun sleep()                    │
│                                    │
│  📦 Dog's own part:                │
│     fun bark()                     │
│     fun fetch()                    │
└────────────────────────────────────┘

Dog object mein DONO cheezein hain!
Kotlin mein open keyword:

DEFAULT: Every Kotlin class is FINAL (inherit nahi kar sakte)
                     ↓
Kyun? "Jaan boojhkar inheritance allow karo" philosophy
                     ↓
`open` likhne se: "Haan, is class ko inherit karo!"
💻 Kotlin Syntax
kotlin
// Parent class — 'open' keyword zaroori!
open class ParentClass(val property: String) {
    
    open fun method() {    // 'open' = override kar sakte ho
        println("Parent method")
    }
    
    fun finalMethod() {   // 'open' nahi = override NAHI kar sakte
        println("This cannot be overridden!")
    }
}

// Child class — ':' se inherit karo
class ChildClass(property: String) : ParentClass(property) {
    
    override fun method() {  // 'override' zaroori!
        super.method()       // Parent ka method bhi chalaao (optional)
        println("Child method")
    }
    
    fun extraMethod() {      // Child ka apna naya method
        println("Only in Child")
    }
}
🧪 Code Examples
Easy Example
kotlin
// Parent class
open class Animal(val name: String, val sound: String) {
    
    // Yeh method sabko milega
    fun eat() {
        println("$name khaana kha raha hai 🍽️")
    }
    
    // Yeh override ho sakta hai
    open fun makeSound() {
        println("$name bol raha hai: $sound")
    }
    
    fun sleep() {
        println("$name so raha hai 😴")
    }
}

// Child classes
class Dog(name: String) : Animal(name, "Woof!") {
    
    override fun makeSound() {
        println("$name bhaunka: WOOF WOOF! 🐕")  // Override!
    }
    
    fun fetch() {
        println("$name ball laa raha hai! 🎾")
    }
}

class Cat(name: String) : Animal(name, "Meow!") {
    
    override fun makeSound() {
        println("$name bol raha hai: Meow~ 🐈")
    }
    
    fun purr() {
        println("$name purr kar raha hai... 😻")
    }
}

class Bird(name: String) : Animal(name, "Tweet!") {
    // makeSound() override nahi kiya — parent ka chalega!
    
    fun fly() {
        println("$name ud raha hai! 🐦")
    }
}

fun main() {
    val dog = Dog("Bruno")
    dog.eat()          // ← Animal se inherited! "Bruno khaana kha raha hai"
    dog.makeSound()    // ← Override! "Bruno bhaunka: WOOF WOOF!"
    dog.fetch()        // ← Dog ka apna! "Bruno ball laa raha hai!"
    dog.sleep()        // ← Animal se inherited!
    
    println()
    
    val bird = Bird("Tweety")
    bird.eat()         // Animal se
    bird.makeSound()   // Parent ka chalega: "Tweety bol raha hai: Tweet!"
    bird.fly()         // Bird ka apna
}
Medium Example — Multi-Level Inheritance
kotlin
// Level 1 — Sabse upar
open class Vehicle(val brand: String, val speed: Int) {
    open fun move() = println("$brand chal raha hai!")
    open fun stop() = println("$brand ruk gaya!")
    fun fuelType() = println("Fuel type define nahi hua")
}

// Level 2 — Vehicle se inherit kiya
open class Car(brand: String, speed: Int, val doors: Int) : Vehicle(brand, speed) {
    override fun move() {
        super.move()  // Parent ka bhi chalate hain
        println("  ↳ Car $doors doors ke saath!")
    }
    
    open fun honk() = println("$brand: Beep! Beep! 📯")
}

// Level 3 — Car se inherit kiya
class ElectricCar(brand: String, speed: Int) : Car(brand, speed, 4) {
    private var battery = 100
    
    override fun move() {
        if (battery > 0) {
            super.move()  // Car ka move chalate hain
            battery -= 10
            println("  🔋 Battery: $battery%")
        } else {
            println("❌ Battery dead! Charge karo pehle!")
        }
    }
    
    override fun fuelType() = println("⚡ Electric!")  // Oh wait, fuelType open nahi!
    // Actually ye Error dega — seekhne ke liye show kiya
    
    fun charge() {
        battery = 100
        println("🔋 $brand fully charged!")
    }
}

fun main() {
    val tesla = ElectricCar("Tesla", 200)
    repeat(11) { tesla.move() }  // 11 baar move karo — 10th pe dead
    tesla.charge()
    tesla.move()  // Ab chalegi!
    tesla.honk()  // Car se inherited!
    tesla.stop()  // Vehicle se inherited!
}
Real-World Android Example
kotlin
// Android mein Base Activity pattern
open class BaseActivity(val activityName: String) {
    
    protected var isRunning = false
    
    // Lifecycle methods — Android jaisi
    open fun onCreate() {
        isRunning = true
        println("[$activityName] onCreate() — Activity shuru hui")
        setupUI()
    }
    
    open fun onResume() {
        println("[$activityName] onResume() — Foreground mein aayi")
    }
    
    open fun onPause() {
        println("[$activityName] onPause() — Background mein gayi")
    }
    
    open fun onDestroy() {
        isRunning = false
        println("[$activityName] onDestroy() — Activity band hui")
    }
    
    private fun setupUI() {
        println("[$activityName] UI setup ho raha hai...")
    }
    
    fun showToast(message: String) {
        println("[$activityName] Toast: '$message'")  // Sab activities use kar sakti
    }
}

class LoginActivity : BaseActivity("LoginActivity") {
    private var loginAttempts = 0
    
    override fun onCreate() {
        super.onCreate()  // ← Zaroori! Parent ka onCreate bhi chalao
        println("[LoginActivity] Login form load ho raha hai")
    }
    
    override fun onResume() {
        super.onResume()
        println("[LoginActivity] Keyboard show ho raha hai")
    }
    
    fun attemptLogin(email: String, password: String) {
        loginAttempts++
        println("[LoginActivity] Login attempt #$loginAttempts: $email")
        
        if (email == "test@gmail.com" && password == "1234") {
            showToast("Login successful!")  // BaseActivity se inherited
        } else {
            showToast("Wrong credentials! Attempt: $loginAttempts")
            if (loginAttempts >= 3) {
                showToast("Account locked!")
            }
        }
    }
}

class HomeActivity : BaseActivity("HomeActivity") {
    private val posts = mutableListOf<String>()
    
    override fun onCreate() {
        super.onCreate()
        loadFeed()
    }
    
    private fun loadFeed() {
        println("[HomeActivity] Feed load ho rahi hai...")
        posts.addAll(listOf("Post 1", "Post 2", "Post 3"))
        println("[HomeActivity] ${posts.size} posts load hue")
    }
    
    fun refreshFeed() {
        println("[HomeActivity] Feed refresh ho rahi hai...")
        showToast("Feed updated!")  // BaseActivity se inherited
    }
}

fun main() {
    println("=== App Chalu ===\n")
    
    val login = LoginActivity()
    login.onCreate()
    login.onResume()
    login.attemptLogin("test@gmail.com", "wrong")
    login.attemptLogin("test@gmail.com", "1234")
    login.onPause()
    login.onDestroy()
    
    println("\n=== Home Screen ===\n")
    
    val home = HomeActivity()
    home.onCreate()
    home.onResume()
    home.refreshFeed()
    home.onDestroy()
}
⚠️ Common Mistakes
kotlin
// ❌ GALTI 1: 'open' bhool gaya
class Animal { fun eat() = println("eating") }
// class Dog : Animal()  ← ERROR! Animal is final!

// ✅ SAHI
open class Animal { open fun eat() = println("eating") }

// ❌ GALTI 2: Constructor chain bhool gaya
open class Person(val name: String)
// class Student : Person  ← ERROR! Person ka constructor call karo

// ✅ SAHI
class Student(name: String, val grade: Int) : Person(name)

// ❌ GALTI 3: Super call bhoolna jab important ho
open class Base {
    open fun setup() {
        println("Important initialization!")
    }
}
class Child : Base() {
    override fun setup() {
        // super.setup()  ← Bhool gaye! Parent ka setup nahi hua
        println("Child setup")
    }
}

// ✅ SAHI
class GoodChild : Base() {
    override fun setup() {
        super.setup()  // Pehle parent, phir apna!
        println("Child setup")
    }
}

// ❌ GALTI 4: Bahut deep inheritance (5+ levels)
// A → B → C → D → E → F ← Confused! Nobody knows what's what
// Max 2-3 levels rakho
💡 Pro Tips
💡 TIP 1: "IS-A" test karo — kya child class parent hai?
   Dog IS-A Animal ✅ (inherit karo)
   Car IS-A Vehicle ✅ (inherit karo)
   Dog IS-A Vehicle ❌ (inherit mat karo!)

💡 TIP 2: super.method() call karo jab parent ka kaam important ho
   Especially lifecycle methods mein (Android)

💡 TIP 3: Max 2-3 levels deep rakho inheritance hierarchy
   Deep hierarchy = confusion = bugs

💡 TIP 4: "Composition over Inheritance" — agar IS-A nahi,
   toh inherit mat karo, compose karo
🎯 When NOT to Use Inheritance
❌ Jab "IS-A" relationship nahi hai:
   Car IS-NOT-A Engine  → Composition use karo
   Stack IS-NOT-A List  → Wrap/Delegate karo

❌ Jab sirf kuch methods chahiye:
   Interface prefer karo

❌ Jab 3+ levels deep jaana pade:
   Rethink your design!

✅ Use karo jab:
   Real IS-A relationship ho
   Common code share karna ho
   Polymorphism chahiye ho
🔥 Interview Questions
Q1: Kotlin mein classes by default final kyun hain?

"Kotlin mein 'Effective Java' principle follow karta hai:
'Design for inheritance or prohibit it.'

Agar class accidentally inherit ho jaye aur koi method
galat override kar de — bugs aayenge jo dhundhna mushkil hai.

open keyword explicitly likhna padta hai — matlab developer
consciously decide kar raha hai ki inheritance allowed hai.

Yeh safer programming practice hai."
Q2: super keyword kya karta hai?

super = Parent class ka reference

Uses:
1. super.method() — Parent ka method call karo
2. super(params) — Parent ka constructor call karo (secondary constructor mein)
3. super<Interface>.method() — Multiple interfaces mein ambiguity resolve karo
🧩 Practice Question
Sawaal: Ek Shape hierarchy banao — Shape parent, Circle aur Rectangle children. Har shape ka area() alag calculate hoga.

kotlin
open class Shape(val color: String) {
    open fun area(): Double = 0.0
    
    fun describe() {
        println("${this::class.simpleName}: Color=$color, Area=${"%.2f".format(area())}")
    }
}

class Circle(color: String, val radius: Double) : Shape(color) {
    override fun area() = Math.PI * radius * radius
}

class Rectangle(color: String, val width: Double, val height: Double) : Shape(color) {
    override fun area() = width * height
}

class Triangle(color: String, val base: Double, val height: Double) : Shape(color) {
    override fun area() = 0.5 * base * height
}

fun main() {
    val shapes = listOf(
        Circle("Red", 5.0),
        Rectangle("Blue", 4.0, 6.0),
        Triangle("Green", 3.0, 8.0)
    )
    
    shapes.forEach { it.describe() }
    
    println("\nSabse bada shape:")
    val largest = shapes.maxByOrNull { it.area() }
    largest?.describe()
}
🧠 Quick Summary + Memory Trick
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  QUICK SUMMARY                                          │
│  • Inheritance = Parent se properties/methods lena     │
│  • open keyword = "Inherit karo mujhse!"               │
│  • override keyword = "Main parent se alag karunga"    │
│  • super = Parent class ka reference                   │
│  • Kotlin classes by default final hain (safe!)        │
│  • IS-A test: Kya child IS-A parent? Toh inherit!      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

🧠 MEMORY TRICK:
"Inheritance = DNA Transfer"
Bachche ko mama-papa ka DNA milta hai (inherited traits)
Plus apni khaas cheezein bhi hoti hain!
open = "Mera DNA transfer hone layak hai"
override = "Main Papa se thoda alag hoon is cheez mein"
🔹 CONCEPT 5: POLYMORPHISM
🧒 Simple Explanation
Poly = Many, Morph = Form → Many Forms (Anek Roop)

Ek hi naam, alag alag kaam!

Socho tumhari teacher "Padho!" kehti hai:

Math teacher = "2+2 karo padho!"
Hindi teacher = "Poem padho!"
Science teacher = "Experiment karo padho!"
Ek hi command ("Padho!"), alag alag response! Yahi hai Polymorphism! 🎭

🌍 Real-Life Example
Remote Control! 📺

Remote ka "Volume Up" button daba:
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  TV connected   → TV volume badhega     │
│  AC connected   → AC fan speed badhegi  │
│  Speaker connected → Speaker louder     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────┘

Same button (makeSound/command),
Alag device (alag class),
Alag result (alag implementation)!
Code mein:

kotlin
fun makeNoise(animal: Animal) {
    animal.makeSound()  // Same call!
    // Dog hoga → "Woof!"
    // Cat hoga → "Meow!"
    // Bird hoga → "Tweet!"
    // Caller ko pata nahi konsa animal hai — bus "makeSound" hai!
}
⚙️ Types of Polymorphism
POLYMORPHISM
├── Compile-Time (Static)
│   └── Method Overloading
│       Same naam + Different parameters
│       Compile time pe decide hota hai
│
└── Runtime (Dynamic)  ← Zyada important!
    └── Method Overriding
        Parent reference + Child object
        Runtime pe decide hota hai
⚙️ Internally — Runtime Polymorphism Kaise Kaam Karta Hai?
JVM ke andar har class ka ek "vtable" hota hai:

Animal vtable:        Dog vtable:
┌─────────────┐       ┌────────────────────┐
│ makeSound() │──────▶│ Dog.makeSound()    │
│ eat()       │──────▶│ Animal.eat()       │
└─────────────┘       └────────────────────┘

Jab likhte ho: val a: Animal = Dog("Bruno")
                              a.makeSound()

JVM checks: "a ka actual type kya hai?" → Dog!
            "Dog ka makeSound() kaunsa hai?" → Dog.makeSound()!
→ Dog.makeSound() chalega!

Yeh DYNAMIC DISPATCH hai!
💻 Kotlin Syntax
kotlin
// ═══════════════════════════════
// 1. COMPILE-TIME — Overloading
// ═══════════════════════════════
class MathHelper {
    fun add(a: Int, b: Int) = a + b              // Int + Int
    fun add(a: Double, b: Double) = a + b         // Double + Double
    fun add(a: Int, b: Int, c: Int) = a + b + c  // 3 numbers
    fun add(a: String, b: String) = a + b         // String concat
}

// ═══════════════════════════════
// 2. RUNTIME — Overriding
// ═══════════════════════════════
open class Animal {
    open fun makeSound() = println("...")
}
class Dog : Animal() {
    override fun makeSound() = println("Woof!")
}
class Cat : Animal() {
    override fun makeSound() = println("Meow!")
}

// Polymorphism in action!
val animals: List<Animal> = listOf(Dog(), Cat(), Dog())
animals.forEach { it.makeSound() }
// Woof! → Meow! → Woof!
// Same call, different results!
🧪 Code Examples
Easy Example — Overloading
kotlin
class Calculator {
    // Same naam "calculate" — alag parameters
    fun calculate(a: Int, b: Int): Int {
        println("Int calculation: $a + $b")
        return a + b
    }
    
    fun calculate(a: Double, b: Double): Double {
        println("Double calculation: $a + $b")
        return a + b
    }
    
    fun calculate(a: Int, b: Int, c: Int): Int {
        println("Three number calculation: $a + $b + $c")
        return a + b + c
    }
    
    fun calculate(values: List<Int>): Int {
        println("List calculation: $values")
        return values.sum()
    }
}

fun main() {
    val calc = Calculator()
    
    println(calc.calculate(5, 3))            // Int version
    println(calc.calculate(5.5, 3.2))        // Double version
    println(calc.calculate(1, 2, 3))         // 3-param version
    println(calc.calculate(listOf(1,2,3,4))) // List version
}
Medium Example — Runtime Polymorphism
kotlin
open class Employee(val name: String, val salary: Double) {
    
    // Override kar sakte hain
    open fun calculateBonus(): Double = salary * 0.10  // Default: 10%
    
    open fun getRole(): String = "Employee"
    
    // Yeh sab ke liye same
    fun generatePayslip() {
        val bonus = calculateBonus()  // ← Yahan magic hoti hai!
        println("━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━")
        println("💼 Payslip: $name (${getRole()})")
        println("   Salary: ₹$salary")
        println("   Bonus: ₹$bonus (${(bonus/salary*100).toInt()}%)")
        println("   Total: ₹${salary + bonus}")
        println("━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━")
    }
}

class Manager(name: String, salary: Double) : Employee(name, salary) {
    override fun calculateBonus() = salary * 0.20  // Managers ko 20%
    override fun getRole() = "Manager"
}

class Intern(name: String, salary: Double) : Employee(name, salary) {
    override fun calculateBonus() = 0.0  // Interns ko bonus nahi 😅
    override fun getRole() = "Intern"
}

class CEO(name: String, salary: Double) : Employee(name, salary) {
    override fun calculateBonus() = salary * 0.50  // CEO ko 50%!
    override fun getRole() = "CEO"
}

fun main() {
    // Sab Employee type ke hain — Polymorphism!
    val team: List<Employee> = listOf(
        CEO("Mukesh", 500000.0),
        Manager("Rahul", 100000.0),
        Employee("Priya", 50000.0),
        Intern("Arjun", 15000.0)
    )
    
    // Ek hi loop — alag alag payslip!
    team.forEach { it.generatePayslip() }
    
    // Total bonus calculation
    val totalBonus = team.sumOf { it.calculateBonus() }
    println("💰 Total bonus distributed: ₹$totalBonus")
}
Real-World Example — Android Payment System
kotlin
// Payment methods — Polymorphism ka perfect use!
abstract class PaymentMethod(val name: String) {
    
    abstract fun processPayment(amount: Double): Boolean
    abstract fun getIcon(): String
    
    // Template method — same for all
    fun pay(amount: Double) {
        println("\n${getIcon()} $name se payment shuru...")
        println("   Amount: ₹$amount")
        
        val success = processPayment(amount)
        
        if (success) {
            println("   ✅ Payment successful!")
            generateReceipt(amount)
        } else {
            println("   ❌ Payment failed!")
        }
    }
    
    private fun generateReceipt(amount: Double) {
        println("   🧾 Receipt: ₹$amount via $name | ${System.currentTimeMillis()}")
    }
}

class UPIPayment(private val upiId: String) : PaymentMethod("UPI") {
    override fun getIcon() = "📱"
    override fun processPayment(amount: Double): Boolean {
        println("   UPI ID: $upiId ko payment bhej raha hai...")
        return upiId.contains("@")  // Simple validation
    }
}

class CreditCardPayment(
    private val cardNumber: String,
    private val limit: Double
) : PaymentMethod("Credit Card") {
    override fun getIcon() = "💳"
    override fun processPayment(amount: Double): Boolean {
        if (amount > limit) {
            println("   Credit limit exceed! Limit: ₹$limit")
            return false
        }
        println("   Card: xxxx-xxxx-xxxx-${cardNumber.takeLast(4)}")
        return true
    }
}

class WalletPayment(private var walletBalance: Double) : PaymentMethod("Wallet") {
    override fun getIcon() = "👛"
    override fun processPayment(amount: Double): Boolean {
        if (amount > walletBalance) {
            println("   Wallet balance kam hai! Balance: ₹$walletBalance")
            return false
        }
        walletBalance -= amount
        println("   Wallet balance remaining: ₹$walletBalance")
        return true
    }
}

// Polymorphic function — koi bhi PaymentMethod accept kare!
fun checkout(payment: PaymentMethod, amount: Double) {
    payment.pay(amount)  // Runtime pe decide hoga konsa pay() chalega
}

fun main() {
    val upi = UPIPayment("rahul@okaxis")
    val card = CreditCardPayment("4111111111111111", 50000.0)
    val wallet = WalletPayment(500.0)
    
    checkout(upi, 1500.0)      // UPI ka processPayment chalega
    checkout(card, 25000.0)    // Card ka processPayment chalega
    checkout(wallet, 1000.0)   // ❌ Balance kam — Wallet ka processPayment
    checkout(wallet, 200.0)    // ✅ Wallet ka processPayment
}
⚠️ Common Mistakes
kotlin
// ❌ GALTI 1: 'override' bhoolna
open class Animal { open fun sound() = println("...") }
class Dog : Animal() {
    // fun sound() = println("Woof!")  ← Warning/Error! override keyword lagao!
    override fun sound() = println("Woof!")  // ✅
}

// ❌ GALTI 2: Non-open method override karna
open class Base {
    fun cantOverride() = println("Final method")
    open fun canOverride() = println("Open method")
}
class Child : Base() {
    // override fun cantOverride() = println("Trying")  ← ERROR!
    override fun canOverride() = println("Overridden")  // ✅
}

// ❌ GALTI 3: Overloading aur Overriding confuse karna
class A {
    open fun show(x: Int) = println("A: $x")
}
class B : A() {
    // Yeh OVERLOADING hai, OVERRIDING nahi!
    fun show(x: String) = println("B: $x")  // Different parameter!
    
    // Yeh OVERRIDING hai!
    override fun show(x: Int) = println("B: $x")  // Same signature!
}
💡 Pro Tips
💡 TIP 1: Parent type mein store karo, child object rakho
   val animals: List<Animal> = listOf(Dog(), Cat(), Bird())
   ← Yahi true polymorphism hai!

💡 TIP 2: 'is' operator se type check karo safely
   if (animal is Dog) { (animal as Dog).fetch() }
   // Smart cast — Kotlin automatically cast karta hai!

💡 TIP 3: 'when' expression ke saath polymorphism
   when (animal) {
       is Dog -> animal.fetch()
       is Cat -> animal.purr()
   }
🔥 Interview Questions
Q1: Polymorphism kya hai? Compile-time aur Runtime mein kya fark?

"Polymorphism matlab 'Many Forms' — ek hi naam, alag behaviors.

Compile-time (Overloading):
- Same class, same method name, different parameters
- Compiler decide karta hai compile time pe konsa method
- Example: add(int,int) vs add(double,double)

Runtime (Overriding):
- Parent-child, same method signature
- JVM decide karta hai RUNTIME pe konsa method chalega
- Example: animal.makeSound() → Dog ya Cat ka chalega?

Runtime polymorphism zyada powerful hai — dynamic behavior!"
Q2: Method overloading Kotlin mein kaise different hai Java se?

Kotlin mein Default parameters hain, isliye overloading
ki zarurat kam padti hai:

// Kotlin way — default parameters!
fun greet(name: String, greeting: String = "Hello") {
    println("$greeting, $name!")
}
greet("Rahul")           // Default: "Hello, Rahul!"
greet("Rahul", "Namaste") // Custom: "Namaste, Rahul!"

Java mein yeh do methods likhne padte:
void greet(String name) { }
void greet(String name, String greeting) { }
🧩 Practice Question
Sawaal: Alag alag Notification types banao (Email, SMS, Push). Sab send() method implement karein. Ek list mein daalo aur sab send karo.

kotlin
abstract class Notification(val recipient: String, val message: String) {
    abstract fun send()
    
    fun validate(): Boolean {
        return recipient.isNotBlank() && message.isNotBlank()
    }
}

class EmailNotification(
    recipient: String, message: String,
    private val subject: String
) : Notification(recipient, message) {
    override fun send() {
        if (!validate()) { println("❌ Invalid email data!"); return }
        println("📧 Email → $recipient")
        println("   Subject: $subject")
        println("   Body: $message")
    }
}

class SMSNotification(
    recipient: String, message: String
) : Notification(recipient, message) {
    override fun send() {
        if (!validate()) { println("❌ Invalid SMS data!"); return }
        println("📱 SMS → $recipient: $message")
    }
}

class PushNotification(
    recipient: String, message: String,
    private val title: String
) : Notification(recipient, message) {
    override fun send() {
        println("🔔 Push → $recipient | $title: $message")
    }
}

fun sendAll(notifications: List<Notification>) {
    println("=== Sending ${notifications.size} notifications ===")
    notifications.forEach { notification ->
        notification.send()  // Polymorphism!
        println()
    }
}

fun main() {
    val notifications: List<Notification> = listOf(
        EmailNotification("rahul@gmail.com", "Aapka order place hua!", "Order Confirmation"),
        SMSNotification("+91-9876543210", "OTP: 123456"),
        PushNotification("device_token_abc", "Flash sale: 50% OFF!", "🔥 Sale Alert!")
    )
    
    sendAll(notifications)
}
🧠 Quick Summary + Memory Trick
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  QUICK SUMMARY                                          │
│  • Polymorphism = Many Forms — ek naam, alag kaam      │
│  • Overloading = Same class, different params           │
│  • Overriding = Parent-child, same signature           │
│  • Runtime polymorphism = Parent type, Child object    │
│  • JVM runtime pe decide karta hai konsa method chalega│
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

🧠 MEMORY TRICK:
"Polymorphism = SWISS ARMY KNIFE"
Ek knife, alag alag tools — scissors, screwdriver, blade
Ek call (animal.sound()), alag results (Dog/Cat/Bird)!
🔹 CONCEPT 6: ABSTRACTION
🧒 Simple Explanation
Abstraction = Andar ki complexity chupaao, sirf important cheezein dikhao

Socho tum Car chalate ho:

TUM JAANTE HO:            TUM NAHI JAANTE:
✅ Steering ghuma →       ❌ Steering column mechanism
   Car mud jaati hai      ❌ Hydraulic fluid kaise kaam karta
✅ Accelerator daba →     ❌ Engine combustion process
   Car tej hoti hai       ❌ Fuel injection timing
✅ Brake daba →           ❌ ABS system ka algorithm
   Car rukti hai          ❌ Brake pad friction calculations

Tumhe complex internals ki zarurat NAHI hai!
Sirf interface (steering, pedals) jaano!
🌍 Real-Life Example
TV Remote! 📺

REMOTE (Abstraction):
┌─────────────────┐
│  Power Button   │  → "TV on karo" (tum sirf yeh jaante ho)
│  Volume +/-     │  → "Awaaz badhao/ghataao"
│  Channel +/-    │  → "Channel badlo"
└─────────────────┘

ANDAR KI COMPLEXITY (Hidden):
- Infrared signal encoding
- TV receiver decoding
- HDMI handshake protocol
- Video decoder pipeline
- Audio amplifier circuit
← Yeh sab hidden hai! Tumhe pata nahi, pata hona bhi nahi chahiye
⚙️ Abstraction Achieve Kaise Karte Hain?
Kotlin mein Abstraction ke 2 tarike:

1. Abstract Class  → Partial abstraction
   (Kuch implemented, kuch nahi)

2. Interface       → Full abstraction
   (Sirf contract, koi state nahi)
🔹 CONCEPT 7: ABSTRACT CLASSES
🧒 Simple Explanation
Abstract class = Incomplete class — directly use nahi ho sakti

Jaise koi bol de: "Main ek general 'Vehicle' hoon" — yeh statement incomplete hai!

Vehicle (Abstract — Incomplete):
❓ Sound kya hai? (Define nahi kiya)
❓ Speed kitni hai? (Define nahi kiya)

Car (Concrete — Complete):
✅ Sound: "Vroom!"
✅ Speed: 0-200 km/h

Bike (Concrete — Complete):
✅ Sound: "Zoom!"
✅ Speed: 0-150 km/h
💻 Kotlin Syntax
kotlin
abstract class AbstractClass {
    // Abstract property — subclass MUST define this
    abstract val type: String
    
    // Abstract method — subclass MUST implement this
    abstract fun doWork()
    abstract fun getResult(): String
    
    // Concrete method — already implemented, subclass gets it for free
    fun showInfo() {
        println("Type: $type | Result: ${getResult()}")
    }
    
    // Template method — calls abstract methods
    fun execute() {
        println("Starting...")
        doWork()           // ← Child ka implementation chalega!
        println("Done: ${getResult()}")
    }
}

class ConcreteClass : AbstractClass() {
    override val type = "Concrete"
    override fun doWork() { println("Working hard!") }
    override fun getResult() = "42"
}
🧪 Code Examples
Medium Example
kotlin
abstract class Animal(val name: String) {
    // Abstract — har animal ka sound alag hoga
    abstract fun makeSound(): String
    abstract fun move(): String
    
    // Concrete — sab animals ke liye same
    val kingdom = "Animalia"
    
    fun breathe() = println("$name saans le raha hai")
    
    fun introduce() {
        println("━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━")
        println("🐾 Naam: $name")
        println("   Kingdom: $kingdom")
        println("   Sound: ${makeSound()}")
        println("   Move: ${move()}")
        println("━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━")
    }
}

class Lion(name: String) : Animal(name) {
    override fun makeSound() = "ROAR! 🦁"
    override fun move() = "Chalta hai zameen par"
    
    fun hunt() = println("$name shikar kar raha hai!")
}

class Eagle(name: String) : Animal(name) {
    override fun makeSound() = "SCREECH! 🦅"
    override fun move() = "Udta hai aasman mein"
    
    fun dive() = println("$name dive kar raha hai!")
}

class Dolphin(name: String) : Animal(name) {
    override fun makeSound() = "Click-click! 🐬"
    override fun move() = "Tairta hai paani mein"
}

fun main() {
    // val a = Animal("test")  // ❌ Abstract — instantiate nahi hoga!
    
    val animals = listOf(
        Lion("Simba"),
        Eagle("Sam"),
        Dolphin("Flipper")
    )
    
    animals.forEach { it.introduce() }
    animals.forEach { it.breathe() }  // Concrete method — sab inherit karte hain
}
Real-World Example — Repository Pattern (Android Architecture)
kotlin
// Android Architecture mein common pattern!
data class Product(val id: Int, val name: String, val price: Double)

abstract class BaseRepository<T> {
    // Abstract — implementation alag hogi (API ya Database)
    abstract fun getAll(): List<T>
    abstract fun getById(id: Int): T?
    abstract fun save(item: T): Boolean
    abstract fun delete(id: Int): Boolean
    
    // Concrete — sab repositories ke liye same logic
    fun exists(id: Int): Boolean = getById(id) != null
    
    fun count(): Int = getAll().size
    
    fun saveAll(items: List<T>): Int {
        return items.count { save(it) }
    }
    
    fun getOrDefault(id: Int, default: T): T {
        return getById(id) ?: default
    }
}

// Local Database implementation
class LocalProductRepository : BaseRepository<Product>() {
    private val db = mutableMapOf<Int, Product>()
    
    override fun getAll(): List<Product> = db.values.toList()
    
    override fun getById(id: Int): Product? = db[id]
    
    override fun save(item: Product): Boolean {
        db[item.id] = item
        println("💾 Saved locally: ${item.name}")
        return true
    }
    
    override fun delete(id: Int): Boolean {
        return db.remove(id)?.let {
            println("🗑️ Deleted from local DB: ${it.name}")
            true
        } ?: false
    }
}

// Remote API implementation
class RemoteProductRepository : BaseRepository<Product>() {
    private val apiCache = mutableMapOf<Int, Product>()
    
    override fun getAll(): List<Product> {
        println("🌐 Fetching from API...")
        return apiCache.values.toList()
    }
    
    override fun getById(id: Int): Product? {
        println("🌐 API call for product $id")
        return apiCache[id]
    }
    
    override fun save(item: Product): Boolean {
        println("🌐 POST to API: ${item.name}")
        apiCache[item.id] = item
        return true
    }
    
    override fun delete(id: Int): Boolean {
        println("🌐 DELETE request for $id")
        return apiCache.remove(id) != null
    }
}

fun main() {
    val localRepo: BaseRepository<Product> = LocalProductRepository()
    
    localRepo.save(Product(1, "Phone", 15000.0))
    localRepo.save(Product(2, "Laptop", 55000.0))
    localRepo.save(Product(3, "Tablet", 25000.0))
    
    println("Total products: ${localRepo.count()}")
    println("Product 2 exists: ${localRepo.exists(2)}")
    println("All: ${localRepo.getAll().map { it.name }}")
    
    localRepo.delete(2)
    println("After delete: ${localRepo.count()}")
    
    // Swap to remote — same code!
    val remoteRepo: BaseRepository<Product> = RemoteProductRepository()
    remoteRepo.save(Product(1, "Earbuds", 2000.0))
    println("Remote count: ${remoteRepo.count()}")
}
⚠️ Common Mistakes
kotlin
// ❌ GALTI 1: Abstract class instantiate karna
abstract class Shape { abstract fun area(): Double }
// val s = Shape()  ← ERROR! Abstract class ka object nahi banta

// ❌ GALTI 2: Saare abstract methods implement na karna
abstract class Vehicle {
    abstract fun start()
    abstract fun stop()
}
// class Car : Vehicle() {
//     override fun start() { }
//     // stop() implement nahi kiya ← ERROR!
// }

// ✅ SAHI — saare implement karo
class Car : Vehicle() {
    override fun start() = println("Car start!")
    override fun stop() = println("Car stop!")
}

// ❌ GALTI 3: Abstract property ka default value dena
abstract class Wrong {
    // abstract val name: String = "Default"  // ← ERROR! Abstract can't have value
    abstract val name: String  // ✅ Subclass dega value
}
🔹 CONCEPT 8: INTERFACES
🧒 Simple Explanation
Interface = Contract / Agreement

Socho tumne ek job application di "Software Developer" ke liye.

Job description mein likha hai:

Must know: coding
Must know: debugging
Must know: testing
Yeh ek CONTRACT hai — company ki taraf se: "Jo bhi is role ke liye apply karega, use yeh sab aana chahiye!"

Interface = Job description / Contract

Jo class implement kare = Employee who fulfills the contract

🌍 Real-Life Example
USB Standard! 🔌

USB Interface Contract:
"Jo bhi USB port se connect hona chahta hai,
usse in 4 pins hone chahiye — power, data+, data-, ground"

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  USB Interface (Contract)                   │
│  connect()                                  │
│  transferData()                             │
│  disconnect()                               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────┘
          ↑              ↑              ↑
    Keyboard         Pendrive         Mouse
    (Implements)     (Implements)     (Implements)

Computer ko pata nahi konsa device hai — sirf USB interface hai!
💻 Kotlin Syntax + Difference from Abstract Class
kotlin
interface Flyable {
    // ═══════════════════════════════════════
    // Property — NO backing field! (Sirf abstract)
    val maxAltitude: Int  // ← Must be overridden
    
    // Abstract method — implement karna zaroori
    fun fly()
    fun land()
    
    // Default implementation — override optional!
    fun checkWeather() {
        println("Weather check kar raha hoon...")  // Default behavior
    }
    // ═══════════════════════════════════════
}

interface Swimmable {
    fun swim()
    fun dive()
}

// Multiple interfaces implement kar sakte hain!
class Duck : Flyable, Swimmable {
    override val maxAltitude = 1000  // Flyable contract fulfill
    override fun fly() = println("Duck ud raha hai! 🦆")
    override fun land() = println("Duck land kar raha hai!")
    override fun swim() = println("Duck tair raha hai!")
    override fun dive() = println("Duck dive kar raha hai!")
}
🧪 Code Examples
Easy Example
kotlin
interface Printable {
    fun print()
    
    // Default method — override optional
    fun preview() {
        println("Preview: ${this::class.simpleName}")
    }
}

interface Saveable {
    fun save(filename: String): Boolean
}

class TextDocument(val content: String) : Printable, Saveable {
    override fun print() {
        println("📄 Printing document:")
        println(content)
    }
    
    override fun save(filename: String): Boolean {
        println("💾 Saving '$filename.txt'...")
        return true
    }
}

class ImageDocument(val imagePath: String) : Printable, Saveable {
    override fun print() {
        println("🖼️ Printing image: $imagePath")
    }
    
    override fun preview() {
        println("🖼️ Image preview: $imagePath (${200}x${300}px)")  // Override!
    }
    
    override fun save(filename: String): Boolean {
        println("💾 Saving image '$filename.jpg'...")
        return true
    }
}

fun printAll(documents: List<Printable>) {
    documents.forEach { doc ->
        doc.preview()
        doc.print()
        println()
    }
}

fun main() {
    val docs = listOf(
        TextDocument("Hello World! Yeh ek text document hai."),
        ImageDocument("/images/profile.jpg"),
        TextDocument("Doosra document")
    )
    
    printAll(docs)  // Polymorphism + Interface!
}
Real-World Android Example — Callbacks + Lifecycle
kotlin
// Android mein yeh patterns bahut common hain!

interface OnClickListener {
    fun onClick(viewId: Int)
    fun onLongClick(viewId: Int): Boolean = false  // Default: no long click
}

interface NetworkListener {
    fun onSuccess(data: String)
    fun onError(errorMessage: String)
    fun onLoading(isLoading: Boolean) { }  // Default: empty
}

interface LifecycleObserver {
    fun onStart() { println("Started") }
    fun onStop() { println("Stopped") }
    fun onDestroy() { }
}

// Screen jo sabko implement kare
class ProductListScreen : OnClickListener, NetworkListener, LifecycleObserver {
    
    private val products = mutableListOf<String>()
    
    // LifecycleObserver
    override fun onStart() {
        println("📱 ProductListScreen: Starting...")
        loadProducts()
    }
    
    override fun onStop() {
        println("📱 ProductListScreen: Stopping...")
    }
    
    // OnClickListener
    override fun onClick(viewId: Int) {
        when (viewId) {
            1 -> println("🛍️ Product tapped — detail page khulega")
            2 -> println("🔍 Search button tapped")
            3 -> println("🛒 Cart button tapped")
            else -> println("Unknown button: $viewId")
        }
    }
    
    override fun onLongClick(viewId: Int): Boolean {
        println("🗑️ Long click — delete option dikhega")
        return true  // Event consumed
    }
    
    // NetworkListener
    override fun onLoading(isLoading: Boolean) {
        println("⏳ Loading: $isLoading")
    }
    
    override fun onSuccess(data: String) {
        products.addAll(data.split(","))
        println("✅ ${products.size} products load hue: $products")
    }
    
    override fun onError(errorMessage: String) {
        println("❌ Error: $errorMessage")
        println("🔄 Retry button show kar raha hoon")
    }
    
    private fun loadProducts() {
        // Simulate network call
        onLoading(true)
        // Network se data aaya
        onSuccess("iPhone,Samsung,OnePlus,Pixel")
        onLoading(false)
    }
}

fun main() {
    val screen = ProductListScreen()
    screen.onStart()
    
    // User interactions
    screen.onClick(1)
    screen.onLongClick(1)
    screen.onClick(3)
    
    screen.onStop()
}
⚠️ Common Mistakes
kotlin
// ❌ GALTI 1: Interface mein state (variable) store karna
interface WrongInterface {
    // var count = 0  ← ERROR! Interface mein backing field nahi hota
}

// ✅ SAHI — Property abstract rakho
interface CorrectInterface {
    var count: Int  // Abstract — implementing class store karegi
}

// ❌ GALTI 2: Interface instantiate karna
interface Flyable { fun fly() }
// val f = Flyable()  ← ERROR!

// ✅ Anonymous object use karo
val f = object : Flyable { override fun fly() = println("Flying!") }

// ❌ GALTI 3: Diamond problem ignore karna
interface A { fun hello() = println("A") }
interface B { fun hello() = println("B") }

class C : A, B {
    // override fun hello() ← Kaunsa? A ka ya B ka? ERROR!
    
    // ✅ Explicitly specify karo
    override fun hello() {
        super<A>.hello()  // A ka chalao
        // ya
        super<B>.hello()  // B ka chalao
        // ya dono!
    }
}
💡 Pro Tips
💡 TIP 1: Interface = "CAN-DO" (capability)
   class Duck : Animal(), Flyable, Swimmable
   Duck IS-AN Animal + CAN Fly + CAN Swim

💡 TIP 2: Interface mein companion object bana sakte ho
   interface Config {
       companion object {
           const val MAX_SIZE = 100
       }
   }

💡 TIP 3: Functional (SAM) interface — lambda se use karo
   fun interface OnClick {
       fun clicked(id: Int)
   }
   val listener = OnClick { id -> println("Clicked: $id") }
🔥 Interview Questions
Q: Abstract class vs Interface kab use karein?

"IS-A relationship + shared state chahiye → Abstract Class
   Dog IS-A Animal + Animals have common properties

CAN-DO capability chahiye → Interface
   Duck CAN fly + CAN swim (multiple capabilities)

Quick rule:
- Ek parent chahiye + common implementation → Abstract class
- Multiple capabilities + contract → Interface
- Kotlin mein mostly interfaces prefer karo"
🧠 Quick Summary + Memory Trick
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  QUICK SUMMARY                                        │
│  • Abstract Class = Incomplete class (kuch defined,  │
│    kuch baaki)                                        │
│  • Interface = Pure contract (kya karna hai, kaise   │
│    nahi)                                              │
│  • Multiple interfaces implement kar sakte hain       │
│  • Interface mein state (stored properties) nahi     │
│  • Abstract class mein constructor ho sakta hai       │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

🧠 MEMORY TRICK:
"Abstract = HALF-BAKED ROTI 🫓"
Kuch kaam hua, kuch baaki — khane layak nahi (instantiate nahi)
Subclass complete kare → puri roti bane!

"Interface = JOB DESCRIPTION 📋"
"Yeh sab karna aana chahiye" — details tum decide karo
🟦 LEVEL 3: KOTLIN SPECIAL FEATURES
🔹 CONCEPT 9: DATA CLASSES
🧒 Simple Explanation
Data class = Sirf data rakhne ke liye special class

Normal class banao aur sab equals/toString likhna pade — boring! 😴

Data class banao aur Kotlin sab automatically de deta hai! 🎁

Normal Class mein tum likhte ho:
✍️ equals()      → Do objects equal hain?
✍️ hashCode()    → Set/Map ke liye hash
✍️ toString()    → Print karne ke liye
✍️ copy()        → Copy banana

Data Class mein:
✨ Sab automatically mil jaata hai!
Sirf 'data' word lagao!
🌍 Real-Life Example
ID Card socho! 🪪

ID Card ka kaam hai sirf data hold karna:
- Name: Rahul Sharma
- ID: 12345
- Department: Engineering

ID card mein koi behavior nahi hota — sirf info!
Data class bilkul aise hi hai — sirf data!
💻 Kotlin Syntax
kotlin
data class Person(
    val name: String,
    val age: Int,
    val email: String
)

// Automatically milega:
// 1. toString(): "Person(name=Rahul, age=25, email=...)"
// 2. equals(): name+age+email compare karta hai
// 3. hashCode(): consistent hash
// 4. copy(): copy banana with changes
// 5. componentN(): destructuring
🧪 Code Examples
kotlin
data class User(
    val id: Int,
    val username: String,
    val email: String,
    val age: Int
)

fun main() {
    val user1 = User(1, "rahul_dev", "rahul@gmail.com", 25)
    val user2 = User(1, "rahul_dev", "rahul@gmail.com", 25)
    val user3 = User(2, "priya_123", "priya@gmail.com", 22)
    
    // ═══════════════════════════════════════
    // 1. toString() — Auto!
    println(user1)
    // Output: User(id=1, username=rahul_dev, email=rahul@gmail.com, age=25)
    
    // ═══════════════════════════════════════
    // 2. equals() — Structural! (Data compare karta hai)
    println(user1 == user2)  // true! (same data)
    println(user1 == user3)  // false! (different data)
    println(user1 === user2) // false! (different objects in memory)
    
    // ═══════════════════════════════════════
    // 3. copy() — Immutable modification!
    val updatedUser = user1.copy(email = "new@gmail.com", age = 26)
    println(updatedUser)  // username same, sirf email aur age change
    println(user1)         // Original unchanged!
    
    // ═══════════════════════════════════════
    // 4. Destructuring!
    val (id, username, email, age) = user1
    println("ID: $id, Name: $username")
    
    // ═══════════════════════════════════════
    // 5. In collections — equals aur hashCode kaam aata hai
    val users = setOf(user1, user2, user3)  // user1 == user2, so only 2 in set!
    println("Unique users: ${users.size}")  // 2
    
    // Map mein key ke roop mein
    val userMap = mapOf(user1 to "Active", user3 to "Inactive")
    println(userMap[user2])  // "Active"! user1 == user2 isliye
}
Real-World — API Response Models
kotlin
// Android mein yeh pattern common hai!
data class ApiResponse<T>(
    val success: Boolean,
    val data: T? = null,
    val message: String = "",
    val statusCode: Int = 200
)

data class LoginRequest(
    val email: String,
    val password: String
)

data class LoginResponse(
    val token: String,
    val userId: Int,
    val expiresIn: Long
)

data class Product(
    val id: Int,
    val name: String,
    val price: Double,
    val imageUrl: String,
    val rating: Float = 0f
)

fun simulateLogin(request: LoginRequest): ApiResponse<LoginResponse> {
    return if (request.email.contains("@") && request.password.length >= 6) {
        ApiResponse(
            success = true,
            data = LoginResponse("jwt_token_abc_xyz", 101, 3600L),
            message = "Login successful!"
        )
    } else {
        ApiResponse(
            success = false,
            message = "Invalid credentials",
            statusCode = 401
        )
    }
}

fun main() {
    val request = LoginRequest("rahul@gmail.com", "mypassword")
    val response = simulateLogin(request)
    
    println(response)  // Full toString!
    
    if (response.success) {
        val loginData = response.data!!
        println("Token: ${loginData.token}")
        println("User ID: ${loginData.userId}")
        
        // Copy pattern — update token
        val refreshedData = loginData.copy(token = "new_refreshed_token")
        println("Refreshed: $refreshedData")
    }
}
⚠️ Common Mistakes
kotlin
// ❌ GALTI 1: Data class ko mutable banana (jab zarurat nahi)
data class WrongUser(var name: String, var email: String)
// Mutable = Equals/HashCode problematic in collections!

// ✅ SAHI — val use karo
data class CorrectUser(val name: String, val email: String)

// ❌ GALTI 2: Data class inherit karna
data class Parent(val x: Int)
// data class Child(x: Int, val y: Int) : Parent(x)  // ← ERROR or Bad!
// Data classes should not be subclassed

// ❌ GALTI 3: Body mein property rakhna (equals mein nahi aayegi)
data class Tricky(val name: String) {
    var nickname = "Unknown"  // ← equals/hashCode mein NAHI aayega!
}

val t1 = Tricky("Rahul").apply { nickname = "Raj" }
val t2 = Tricky("Rahul").apply { nickname = "Dev" }
println(t1 == t2)  // TRUE! nickname compare nahi hua!
💡 Pro Tips
💡 TIP 1: Data classes ke liye val prefer karo — immutable = safe
💡 TIP 2: copy() se "immutable update" karo
   val updated = user.copy(email = "new@email.com")
💡 TIP 3: Destructuring ka fayda uthao:
   val (name, age) = user
💡 TIP 4: API response models hamesha data class banao
💡 TIP 5: Data class ko 'open' mat banao — inheritance avoid karo
🧠 Memory Trick
"DATA CLASS = ID CARD 🪪"
ID card sirf data hold karta hai
Kotlin sab automation de deta hai
val use karo — ID card change nahi hota!
🔹 CONCEPT 10: SEALED CLASSES
🧒 Simple Explanation
Sealed class = Closed family — sirf defined members hain

Socho Traffic Light 🚦:

Traffic Light ke sirf 3 states hain:
✅ RED    → Ruko
✅ YELLOW → Prepare karo
✅ GREEN  → Chalo

Koi aur state POSSIBLE NAHI hai!
(PINK light nahi hoti!)

Sealed class = "Yeh sab possible states hain, aur koi nahi!"
🌍 Real-Life Example
Parcel delivery status:

Delivery Status:
📦 Ordered     → "Order place hua"
🏭 Processing  → "Pack ho raha hai"
🚚 Shipped     → "Raaste mein" + tracking number
✅ Delivered   → "Delivered!"
❌ Cancelled   → "Cancel ho gaya" + reason

Koi aur status possible nahi!
Isko Sealed class se represent karein!
💻 Kotlin Syntax
kotlin
sealed class Result<out T> {
    data class Success<T>(val data: T) : Result<T>()
    data class Error(val message: String, val code: Int = -1) : Result<Nothing>()
    object Loading : Result<Nothing>()
    object Empty : Result<Nothing>()
}

// Use karna:
fun handleResult(result: Result<String>) {
    when (result) {
        is Result.Success -> println("Data: ${result.data}")
        is Result.Error   -> println("Error ${result.code}: ${result.message}")
        is Result.Loading -> println("Loading...")
        is Result.Empty   -> println("Koi data nahi!")
        // 'else' ki zarurat NAHI — compiler jaanta hai sab cases!
    }
}
🧪 Code Examples
Real-World — UI State Management (Android Pattern)
kotlin
// Yeh pattern Android apps mein hamesha use hota hai!
sealed class UiState<out T> {
    object Idle : UiState<Nothing>()
    object Loading : UiState<Nothing>()
    data class Success<T>(val data: T) : UiState<T>()
    data class Error(
        val message: String,
        val canRetry: Boolean = true
    ) : UiState<Nothing>()
}

sealed class UserAction {
    object Refresh : UserAction()
    data class Search(val query: String) : UserAction()
    data class SelectItem(val id: Int) : UserAction()
    object Logout : UserAction()
}

// ViewModel jaisi class
class ProductViewModel {
    private var state: UiState<List<String>> = UiState.Idle
    
    private fun render() {
        when (val s = state) {
            is UiState.Idle -> println("🔵 Idle — Kuch nahi ho raha")
            is UiState.Loading -> println("⏳ Loading spinner dikh raha hai...")
            is UiState.Success -> {
                println("✅ ${s.data.size} products show ho rahe hain:")
                s.data.forEach { println("   • $it") }
            }
            is UiState.Error -> {
                println("❌ Error: ${s.message}")
                if (s.canRetry) println("   🔄 Retry button dikh raha hai")
            }
        }
    }
    
    fun handleAction(action: UserAction) {
        when (action) {
            is UserAction.Refresh -> loadProducts()
            is UserAction.Search -> searchProducts(action.query)
            is UserAction.SelectItem -> println("📱 Item ${action.id} select hua")
            is UserAction.Logout -> println("👋 Logout ho raha hai")
        }
    }
    
    private fun loadProducts() {
        state = UiState.Loading
        render()
        
        // Simulate API call
        state = UiState.Success(listOf("iPhone 15", "Samsung S24", "OnePlus 12"))
        render()
    }
    
    private fun searchProducts(query: String) {
        state = UiState.Loading
        render()
        
        val results = listOf("iPhone 15", "Samsung S24", "OnePlus 12")
            .filter { it.contains(query, ignoreCase = true) }
        
        state = if (results.isEmpty()) UiState.Empty
                else UiState.Success(results)
        render()
    }
}

// Can't instantiate outside: UiState.Empty and UiState.Loading are objects
// Success and Error are data classes

fun main() {
    val vm = ProductViewModel()
    
    vm.handleAction(UserAction.Refresh)
    println()
    
    vm.handleAction(UserAction.Search("samsung"))
    println()
    
    vm.handleAction(UserAction.Search("apple laptop"))  // Empty result
    println()
    
    vm.handleAction(UserAction.SelectItem(2))
}
⚠️ Common Mistakes
kotlin
// ❌ GALTI 1: Sealed class ko alag file mein extend karna (Kotlin 1.5 se pehle)
// sealed class Result (Result.kt mein)
// class CustomResult : Result() (DifferentFile.kt mein) ← Pehle nahi tha allowed

// ✅ Ab Kotlin 1.5+ mein same package mein kar sakte hain

// ❌ GALTI 2: else branch likhna (zarurat nahi + dangerous!)
fun handleResult(result: Result<String>) {
    when (result) {
        is Result.Success -> println("Success!")
        else -> println("Something else")  // ← Naya type add karo, yeh handle ho jaayega
        // Lekin silently! Bug!
    }
}

// ✅ SAHI — Sab explicitly handle karo (no else!)
fun handleResultCorrect(result: Result<String>) {
    when (result) {
        is Result.Success -> println("Success: ${result.data}")
        is Result.Error   -> println("Error: ${result.message}")
        is Result.Loading -> println("Loading...")
        is Result.Empty   -> println("Empty!")
        // Compiler ensure karega — agar naya type aaya toh ERROR yahan aayega!
    }
}
🧠 Memory Trick
"SEALED CLASS = CLOSED PANDORA'S BOX 📦"
Box mein sirf defined cheezein hain — koi aur nahi!
when expression mein sab cases cover karo — else nahi!
Naya type add karo → Compiler sab jagah batayega!
🔹 CONCEPT 11: ENUM CLASSES
🧒 Simple Explanation
Enum = Fixed set of named constants

Socho Days of Week 📅:

Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, Friday, Saturday, Sunday

Ye 7 options fix hain — na badal sakte, na naye add hote
Isi ke liye ENUM!
💻 Kotlin Syntax + Examples
kotlin
// Simple Enum
enum class Direction { NORTH, SOUTH, EAST, WEST }

// Enum with properties + methods
enum class Planet(val mass: Double, val radius: Double) {
    MERCURY(3.303e+23, 2.4397e6),
    VENUS(4.869e+24, 6.0518e6),
    EARTH(5.976e+24, 6.37814e6),
    MARS(6.421e+23, 3.3972e6);
    
    // Surface gravity calculate karo
    fun surfaceGravity(): Double {
        val G = 6.67300E-11
        return G * mass / (radius * radius)
    }
    
    fun surfaceWeight(otherMass: Double) = otherMass * surfaceGravity()
}

// Real-world enum
enum class OrderStatus(val displayName: String, val emoji: String) {
    PENDING("Pending", "🕐"),
    CONFIRMED("Confirmed", "✅"),
    PROCESSING("Processing", "⚙️"),
    SHIPPED("Shipped", "🚚"),
    DELIVERED("Delivered", "📦"),
    CANCELLED("Cancelled", "❌");
    
    val isFinal: Boolean
        get() = this == DELIVERED || this == CANCELLED
    
    fun canCancel(): Boolean = this == PENDING || this == CONFIRMED
    
    companion object {
        fun fromString(value: String): OrderStatus? {
            return values().find { it.name == value.uppercase() }
        }
    }
}

fun main() {
    // Basic usage
    val dir = Direction.NORTH
    println(dir)           // NORTH
    println(dir.name)      // "NORTH"
    println(dir.ordinal)   // 0 (index)
    
    // Enum in when
    val message = when (dir) {
        Direction.NORTH -> "Upar jao ⬆️"
        Direction.SOUTH -> "Neeche jao ⬇️"
        Direction.EAST  -> "Daayein jao ➡️"
        Direction.WEST  -> "Baayein jao ⬅️"
    }
    println(message)
    
    // OrderStatus
    val status = OrderStatus.SHIPPED
    println("${status.emoji} ${status.displayName}")
    println("Final hai kya: ${status.isFinal}")
    println("Cancel ho sakta: ${status.canCancel()}")
    
    // All values iterate karo
    println("\nSab order statuses:")
    OrderStatus.values().forEach { s ->
        println("  ${s.emoji} ${s.displayName} — Final: ${s.isFinal}")
    }
    
    // Planet example
    val earthWeight = 75.0  // 75 kg on Earth
    val mass = earthWeight / Planet.EARTH.surfaceGravity()
    Planet.values().forEach { p ->
        println("Weight on ${p.name}: ${"%.2f".format(p.surfaceWeight(mass))} kg")
    }
    
    // String se enum
    val found = OrderStatus.fromString("shipped")
    println("\nFound: $found")  // SHIPPED
}
🧠 Memory Trick
"ENUM = MENU 📋"
Restaurant menu mein fixed items hain — Monday, Tuesday...
Naya item baad mein nahi aata randomly!
Values fix, known, limited!
🔹 CONCEPT 12: COMPANION OBJECT
🧒 Simple Explanation
Companion Object = Class ke saath attached ek special object

Jaise school mein Principal ka ek Personal Assistant hota hai:

SCHOOL (Class)
└── PRINCIPAL (Object)
    └── PA (Companion Object)
        - Sabka schedule manage kare
        - Visitors receive kare
        - Important info store kare

PA = Hamesha Principal ke saath, sab log directly PA se baat karte hain
🌍 Real-Life Example
Factory ka Quality Control Officer:

Car Factory (Class)
└── QC Officer (Companion Object)
    - Standards define karta hai
    - Har car check karta hai
    - Factory-level data rakhta hai

Object bane bina Factory.QCOfficer.check() call kar sakte hain!
💻 Kotlin Syntax
kotlin
class MyClass {
    companion object {
        // Class-level constants
        const val MAX_SIZE = 100
        
        // Factory methods
        fun create(): MyClass = MyClass()
        
        // Class-level state
        private var instanceCount = 0
        fun getCount() = instanceCount
    }
    
    init {
        companion.instanceCount++  // ya sirf instanceCount++
    }
}

// Use karna — object banaye bina!
val obj = MyClass.create()
println(MyClass.MAX_SIZE)
println(MyClass.getCount())
🧪 Code Examples
kotlin
class User private constructor(   // ← private constructor!
    val id: Int,
    val username: String,
    val email: String,
    val role: String
) {
    companion object {
        // Counter — kitne users bane
        private var userCount = 0
        
        // Roles
        const val ROLE_ADMIN = "ADMIN"
        const val ROLE_USER = "USER"
        const val ROLE_GUEST = "GUEST"
        
        // Factory methods — sirf yahi se User bana sakte ho!
        fun createAdmin(username: String, email: String): User {
            userCount++
            return User(userCount, username, email, ROLE_ADMIN)
        }
        
        fun createRegularUser(username: String, email: String): User {
            userCount++
            return User(userCount, username, email, ROLE_USER)
        }
        
        fun createGuest(): User {
            userCount++
            return User(userCount, "Guest_$userCount", "guest@temp.com", ROLE_GUEST)
        }
        
        fun getTotalUsers() = userCount
    }
    
    val isAdmin: Boolean get() = role == ROLE_ADMIN
    
    override fun toString() = "[$role] $username ($email)"
}

fun main() {
    // val u = User(1, "test", "test@test.com", "USER")  // ❌ Private constructor!
    
    val admin = User.createAdmin("superadmin", "admin@company.com")
    val user1 = User.createRegularUser("rahul", "rahul@gmail.com")
    val user2 = User.createRegularUser("priya", "priya@gmail.com")
    val guest = User.createGuest()
    
    println(admin)   // [ADMIN] superadmin (admin@company.com)
    println(user1)   // [USER] rahul (rahul@gmail.com)
    println(guest)   // [GUEST] Guest_4 (guest@temp.com)
    
    println("Total users: ${User.getTotalUsers()}")  // 4
    println("Is admin: ${admin.isAdmin}")             // true
    println("Max role: ${User.ROLE_ADMIN}")           // ADMIN
}
🧠 Memory Trick
"COMPANION OBJECT = SECRETARY 📱"
Boss (Class) ke saath hamesha secretary hoti hai!
Secretary se baat karo bina boss se mile!
User.createAdmin() = Boss ke saath seedha baat kiye bina kaam!
Java ka 'static' = Kotlin ka 'companion object'
🔹 CONCEPT 13: OBJECT (SINGLETON)
🧒 Simple Explanation
Object keyword = Singleton — Sirf ek hi instance

Socho Sun ☀️:

Poori duniya mein sirf ek hi sun hai!
Sab log same sun se roshni lete hain!
Naya sun nahi banta!

Singleton = Sirf ek instance — sab share karte hain!
🌍 Real-Life Example
Government Registry Office:

National ID Registry (Singleton):
- Sirf ek hi office hai poore desh mein
- Sab log wahaan jaate hain
- Ek hi master database hai

AppConfig (Singleton):
- Sirf ek hi config hogi pure app mein
- Sab classes same config access karein
- Naya config object mat banao!
💻 Kotlin Syntax
kotlin
// Singleton object — 'object' keyword!
object AppConfig {
    const val APP_NAME = "MyApp"
    const val VERSION = "2.0.0"
    var isDebugMode = false
    
    fun getBaseUrl(): String {
        return if (isDebugMode) "http://localhost:8080" 
               else "https://api.production.com"
    }
}

// Use karna — no instantiation!
println(AppConfig.APP_NAME)
AppConfig.isDebugMode = true
println(AppConfig.getBaseUrl())

// ═══════════════════════════════════════
// Anonymous Object — ek baar use ke liye
// ═══════════════════════════════════════
val clickListener = object : OnClickListener {
    override fun onClick(id: Int) {
        println("Clicked: $id")
    }
}
🧪 Real-World Example
kotlin
// App-wide Database Manager — Singleton!
object DatabaseManager {
    private val database = mutableMapOf<String, MutableList<String>>()
    private var isConnected = false
    
    fun connect() {
        if (!isConnected) {
            println("🔗 Database se connect ho raha hai...")
            isConnected = true
            println("✅ Connected!")
        } else {
            println("ℹ️ Already connected!")
        }
    }
    
    fun insert(table: String, value: String) {
        require(isConnected) { "Pehle connect karo!" }
        database.getOrPut(table) { mutableListOf() }.add(value)
        println("✅ '$value' insert hua '$table' mein")
    }
    
    fun getAll(table: String): List<String> {
        return database[table]?.toList() ?: emptyList()
    }
    
    fun disconnect() {
        isConnected = false
        println("🔌 Disconnected!")
    }
}

// Logger Singleton
object Logger {
    private val logs = mutableListOf<String>()
    
    fun log(tag: String, message: String) {
        val entry = "[$tag] $message"
        logs.add(entry)
        println("📝 $entry")
    }
    
    fun getLogs() = logs.toList()
    fun clearLogs() = logs.clear()
}

fun main() {
    // Connect once — same instance everywhere!
    DatabaseManager.connect()
    DatabaseManager.connect()  // Already connected!
    
    DatabaseManager.insert("users", "Rahul Sharma")
    DatabaseManager.insert("users", "Priya Singh")
    DatabaseManager.insert("products", "iPhone 15")
    
    println("Users: ${DatabaseManager.getAll("users")}")
    println("Products: ${DatabaseManager.getAll("products")}")
    
    Logger.log("MainActivity", "App started")
    Logger.log("LoginActivity", "User logged in")
    
    println("\nAll logs: ${Logger.getLogs()}")
    
    DatabaseManager.disconnect()
}
🧠 Memory Trick
"OBJECT = SUN ☀️"
Poori duniya mein sirf ek sun!
Poore app mein sirf ek AppConfig!
New nahi banta — hamesha wohi ek!
Thread-safe automatically in Kotlin!
🔹 CONCEPT 14: DELEGATION
🧒 Simple Explanation
Delegation = Kaam kisi aur ko de do!

Socho CEO 👔:

CEO → (delegates) → Manager → (delegates) → Team Lead → (delegates) → Developer

CEO kehta hai "App banao!"
Manager kehta hai "Feature implement karo!"
Team Lead kehta hai "Yeh task karo!"
Developer actually karta hai!

CEO directly code nahi likhta — DELEGATE karta hai!
💻 Kotlin Syntax
kotlin
// Interface delegation — 'by' keyword!
interface Logger {
    fun log(message: String)
    fun logError(message: String)
}

class ConsoleLogger : Logger {
    override fun log(message: String) = println("ℹ️ $message")
    override fun logError(message: String) = println("❌ ERROR: $message")
}

// UserService ne Logger ka kaam ConsoleLogger ko delegate kiya!
class UserService(logger: Logger = ConsoleLogger()) : Logger by logger {
    // Logger ke saare methods automatically ConsoleLogger ko jayenge!
    
    fun createUser(name: String) {
        log("User create ho raha hai: $name")  // Delegates to ConsoleLogger!
        // create logic
        log("User create ho gaya: $name")
    }
}

// Property delegation!
class MyClass {
    // 'lazy' bhi ek delegate hai!
    val expensiveData by lazy {
        println("Computing...")
        "Computed Result"
    }
    
    // Observable delegate
    var name: String by Delegates.observable("") { property, old, new ->
        println("${property.name}: '$old' → '$new'")
    }
}
🧪 Real-World Example
kotlin
import kotlin.properties.Delegates

class SettingsManager {
    // Observable — koi bhi change hone pe notification!
    var darkMode: Boolean by Delegates.observable(false) { _, old, new ->
        println("🌗 Dark mode: $old → $new")
        onSettingsChanged("darkMode", new)
    }
    
    var fontSize: Int by Delegates.observable(14) { _, old, new ->
        println("🔠 Font size: $old → $new")
        onSettingsChanged("fontSize", new)
    }
    
    // Vetoable — change reject kar sakte ho!
    var volume: Int by Delegates.vetoable(50) { _, old, new ->
        val accepted = new in 0..100
        if (!accepted) println("❌ Volume $new invalid! 0-100 ke beech hona chahiye")
        accepted  // true = accept, false = reject
    }
    
    private fun onSettingsChanged(key: String, value: Any) {
        println("💾 '$key' = '$value' save ho raha hai...")
    }
}

fun main() {
    val settings = SettingsManager()
    
    settings.darkMode = true    // Change notification aayega
    settings.fontSize = 18      // Change notification
    
    settings.volume = 75        // ✅ Accepted (0-100 range)
    settings.volume = 150       // ❌ Rejected! Volume stays 75
    settings.volume = -10       // ❌ Rejected!
    println("Final volume: ${settings.volume}")  // 75
